Intervals are closed rational endpoints; enclosures need an independently
checkable certificate for the enclosed exact operation and its unitary
or completely-positive trace-preserving property. An unverified box of
numbers alone is not an operation model.

For each admitted total control count, control_models binds the actual
controlled implementation, including environment/measurement conventions.
A unitary model may use the exact controlled-U construction including its
global phase. A channel is not given a coherent controlled extension by
controlling arbitrary Kraus operators; it requires an explicit instrument
or dilation/control model. The checker resolves every parameter and
control context and constructs the realised operation from that model,
checks dimensions and normalization, and verifies the claimed distance
with §12.1's interval algorithm. The epsilon field remains a separate
accuracy/matching obligation and is not the operator definition.
Exact entries may omit the model and then denote §4.4's exact opcode,
including its exact controlled form. A supplied model must agree with
that contract. No absent model licenses ideal-vs-ideal self-comparison.
```

For legacy targets lacking a model, preserve parse/execution compatibility
but record affected numerical/channel obligations as unchecked (missing
realisation model). Do not issue a checked executable certificate from
those missing obligations. The new model version is an explicit target
format extension; unknown model versions receive unsupported-model
reporting, never a guessed matrix.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

New native-model bytes change target_hash and every target-bound schedule
and certificate. No canonical source or reference catalog changes. Target
authors, scheduler and checker must resolve the same model and controlled
variant; a simulator using ideal opcodes alone is not a physical native
model checker. Physical stochastic failure probabilities remain separate
from logical approximation epsilon (see fd0).

## Conformance vectors and verification

Use X/−X with an outer control and compare matrix units/Choi operators;
test uncontrolled equal channels but unequal controlled processes. Exact
X without a model remains valid. Missing approximate model, wrong arity,
non-normalized Kraus list, parameter/control mismatch, a model-hash change
and a rigorously inconclusive interval claim must not pass the obligation.
The Bell-state sign witness is checked in
[contract-verification](2026-11-02-contract-verification.md). No native
target checker was executed for this note.

## Release and after acceptance

Recommend a **0.9 K contract repair with a versioned model schema**, or a
0.10 target extension with this current gap explicitly blocking independent
approximation claims. The checker question is already the cross-repo bead
filing request; the orchestrator should file/retain it in this repo with
this exact missing mechanism. Council must accept the model and migration
rules before a consumer advertises checked approximate native support.

---
name: beads
description: Use when working in a repository that uses bd or Beads for durable project task tracking, issue dependencies, blocker management, multi-session handoff, or shared work memory. Trigger when the user asks to find ready work, claim or close tasks, create follow-up work, inspect blockers, recover project context, or choose between local planning and persistent project tracking.
---

# Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

# 🚨 SESSION CLOSE PROTOCOL 🚨

**CRITICAL**: Before saying "done" or "complete", you MUST run this checklist:

```
[ ] 1. bd close <id1> <id2> ...   (close completed issues)
[ ] 2. run quality gates        (tests, linters, builds when relevant)
[ ] 3. git status               (check what changed)
[ ] 4. follow active profile    (conservative: report handoff; team-maintainer: commit/sync/push if enabled)
```

**Policy:** Conservative is the default. Commit, sync, or push only when the active user, orchestrator, or repository profile grants that authority.

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

## Common Workflows

**Starting work:**
```bash
bd ready           # Find available work
bd show <id>       # Review issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim it
```

**Completing work:**
```bash
bd close <id1> <id2> ...    # Close all completed issues at once
git status                  # Check changed files
# Conservative/minimal/default: report status and proposed commands; wait for approval
# Team-maintainer opt-in only, unless current instructions forbid it:
# git add . && git commit -m "..."
# git push
```

**Creating dependent work:**
```bash
# Run bd create commands in parallel (use subagents for many items)
bd create --title="Implement feature X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=feature
bd create --title="Write tests for X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task
bd dep add beads-yyy beads-xxx  # Tests depend on Feature (Feature blocks tests)
```

> **Note**: AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md are independent files (not symlinked and not sharing an inode). Mirror substantive edits across both, or symlink one to the other.

.beads/issues.jsonl:19:{"_type":"issue","id":"b-isa-spec-v2v","title":"§12.3 per-context frame descriptors (frame_contexts) for mixed-class realised contexts (from bennett-sched)","description":"Source: bennett-sched/.tmp/cross-repo-beads.md, \"Upstream request: per-context frame descriptors\". Local owner bennett-sched.\n\nDetermination: a legal 0.9 root pair has two wire[1] mut slots on classes A and B (capacity one each) and calls the same linked one-wire flip closure on each. The callee has two realised contexts with equal closure/variant/control-count keys and different resolved mut:0 classes. §12.3 has one region record per closure hash and one required §9.4 frame; each slot record has one concrete class and no context selector. Neither a class wildcard nor duplicate mut:0 slots is admitted, so one descriptor cannot describe both invocations. This blocks a complete certificate for a valid mixed-class request, independently of bennett-sched-enz.\n\nEvidence: bennett-sched tests/pipeline.rs::context_varying_frame_classes_are_explicitly_deferred_instead_of_misreported.\n\nProposed exact normative text replacing the required frame entry in §12.3: \"Each region record MUST contain exactly one of frame or frame_contexts. frame is the §9.4 descriptor shared by every realised invocation context of that region. frame_contexts is an array of {context: \u003cfully resolved §12.1 invocation context key\u003e, frame: \u003c§9.4 descriptor\u003e}, in increasing bytewise order of JCS context encodings. ../bennett/docs/council/isa-09-k82-2026-11-01/round-1/doc.md:993:| `qinplace` | `qinplace @g @ginv tk x (ins..) (mem..) (args..)`; written `qinplace @g @ginv tk x` when all three lists are empty (and only then) | **Well-formed** iff `@g` and `@ginv` are oracles whose `in` slots are x's width n followed by the widths of `ins` in order, whose `tgt` width is n, whose fault widths are equal (F), whose `mem` slots match the `mem` regions (role `in`, §3.3's matching rule) and whose `args` types are those of `args`; x, the `ins` wires and the effective control context are pairwise disjoint. **Sem**: `t = qalloc any n; f = qalloc any F; oracle_call @g tk (x, ins..) t f (mem..) (args..); qexch x t; oracle_call @ginv tk (x, ins..) t f (mem..) (args..); qfree t; qfree f`, so x ← g(x; ins, mem, args); these are its generated operations, in this order (§11). **Inverse-pair obligation**: under the tuple of `tk`, the call's `args` values and the tuples of the tokens among them, on every basis value of x, of the `ins` wires and of the `mem` cells, both oracles are fault-free and g(·; ins, mem, args) and ginv(·; ins, mem, args) are mutually inverse permutations of the n-bit encoding. It is carried by an `inverse_pairs` entry (§12.3) of the certificate record of the region whose body contains the `qinplace`; an entry `{g: G, ginv: H}` covers both `qinplace @G @H` and `qinplace @H @G` (its claim checks both compositions). An entry **covers** a `qinplace` when its `budget` is componentwise ≤ the tuple of `tk` (success at a tuple implies the same result at every larger one, §5.4), every token-typed argument's tuple equals the entry's `arg_tokens` tuple for that argument (equality: a nested oracle's outcome may change with its budget), and every other `args` value lies in its claim's `args` domain. Its claim is an `exhaustive` claim with `expected: "inverse_pair"`, which the executor verifies as a checker does (once per closure hash, before first use), or a `structural` `inverse_pair_library` claim, which covers when the named claim of the linked library's certificate is such a claim for the same closure hashes of g and ginv (no re-execution; a library claim without an `args` domain covers every value; its proof or evidence label is the library claim's); a `sampled` claim never covers. Before any effect of a CC/R region call, every `qinplace` the callee executes (transitively; its token and `args` are resolved from the call's classical arguments, which reach lifted calls only as classical arguments, §4.2) MUST have a covering entry, else CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP`. The private t and f are not values of the region for §6: the certified pair cleans them, the only cleanup that is not a §6.4 realisation. Under control the allocations are unconditional and every gate of both calls and of the exchange is controlled |

../bennett/docs/council/isa-09-k82-2026-11-01/round-1/prompt.md:2182:| `qinplace` | `qinplace @g @ginv tk x (ins..) (mem..) (args..)`; written `qinplace @g @ginv tk x` when all three lists are empty (and only then) | **Well-formed** iff `@g` and `@ginv` are oracles whose `in` slots are x's width n followed by the widths of `ins` in order, whose `tgt` width is n, whose fault widths are equal (F), whose `mem` slots match the `mem` regions (role `in`, §3.3's matching rule) and whose `args` types are those of `args`; x, the `ins` wires and the effective control context are pairwise disjoint. **Sem**: `t = qalloc any n; f = qalloc any F; oracle_call @g tk (x, ins..) t f (mem..) (args..); qexch x t; oracle_call @ginv tk (x, ins..) t f (mem..) (args..); qfree t; qfree f`, so x ← g(x; ins, mem, args); these are its generated operations, in this order (§11). **Inverse-pair obligation**: under the tuple of `tk`, the call's `args` values and the tuples of the tokens among them, on every basis value of x, of the `ins` wires and of the `mem` cells, both oracles are fault-free and g(·; ins, mem, args) and ginv(·; ins, mem, args) are mutually inverse permutations of the n-bit encoding. It is carried by an `inverse_pairs` entry (§12.3) of the certificate record of the region whose body contains the `qinplace`; an entry `{g: G, ginv: H}` covers both `qinplace @G @H` and `qinplace @H @G` (its claim checks both compositions). An entry **covers** a `qinplace` when its `budget` is componentwise ≤ the tuple of `tk` (success at a tuple implies the same result at every larger one, §5.4), every token-typed argument's tuple equals the entry's `arg_tokens` tuple for that argument (equality: a nested oracle's outcome may change with its budget), and every other `args` value lies in its claim's `args` domain. Its claim is an `exhaustive` claim with `expected: "inverse_pair"`, which the executor verifies as a checker does (once per closure hash, before first use), or a `structural` `inverse_pair_library` claim, which covers when the named claim of the linked library's certificate is such a claim for the same closure hashes of g and ginv (no re-execution; a library claim without an `args` domain covers every value; its proof or evidence label is the library claim's); a `sampled` claim never covers. Before any effect of a CC/R region call, every `qinplace` the callee executes (transitively; its token and `args` are resolved from the call's classical arguments, which reach lifted calls only as classical arguments, §4.2) MUST have a covering entry, else CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP`. The private t and f are not values of the region for §6: the certified pair cleans them, the only cleanup that is not a §6.4 realisation. Under control the allocations are unconditional and every gate of both calls and of the exchange is controlled |

docs/notes/2026-10-29-isa-09-evidence.md:49:`already-realised` concerns observable software capability. `gap` identifies the precise missing contract, not an impossibility of universal simulation. Finite enumeration can express surprisingly much; absence of one opcode does not prove absence of every construction. `efficiency` retains the 0.8 construction and defers an optional primitive. The quantitative comparisons below count reference rows separately from expanded gates and physical wires. A Q oracle call with budget B has B forward and B inverse machine steps plus commit/initialisation; its reference interpreter executes at most B rows. A lifted callee oracle is **one caller row**, but its own full circuit is charged too. CC/R frames are classical history, not B quantum frames.
docs/notes/2026-10-29-isa-09-evidence.md:84:Add `"qreg[*]"` to the type grammar/JCS type strings. Add these opcodes to their tier/schema tables; faults use code 4, allocation failure uses the existing allocation-error convention clarified by ISA-7. No new numeric fault code. Allocation and reference frames retain the pre-state and are reversed once under §7.4. Literal slices may also take a runtime `lo:i64`; bounds are evaluated without wrap. A schedule/certificate binds the **concrete** widths, arguments, budget, variant and inherited controls at the call, and is validated then; a width mismatch cannot be excused by an existing static schedule. Runtime scheduling sees classical counts only, satisfying §3.1. Schemas must distinguish the static type `qreg[*]` from the realised slot dimensions; the latter are not wildcards in a gate schedule.
docs/notes/2026-10-29-isa-09-evidence.md:108:With tokens B=2 and B=3, §16.3 derives respectively `(t,f)→(t,f⊕1)` and `(t⊕(x+1 mod 256),f)`. Measuring some earlier register to choose b changes nothing about this legality. For K tuple alternatives, pre-mint K tokens and emit O(K) leaves and O(log K) balanced comparisons. Indeed uint64 components have a finite domain, at most 2^256 tuples before validation: an absolute “no candidate realisation” claim is false if exhaustive pre-minting is admitted. **The genuine contract gap is uniform in-device token creation without requiring that enumeration or returning to a host service.** There is no numerical law in 0.8 rounding B up to a power of two; doing so changes F_B.
docs/notes/2026-10-29-isa-09-evidence.md:120:**Verdict: gap — uniform device minting; finite dispatch is already realised; no rung depends on minting.**
docs/notes/2026-10-29-isa-09-evidence.md:203:**Verdict: already-realised — R budget instrumentation; exact named exhaustion uses ISA-7; no extra rung blocker.**
docs/notes/2026-10-29-isa-09-evidence.md:258:**Verdict: already-realised — destructive measure plus exact re-preparation reproduces the instrument; no rung blocker.**
docs/notes/2026-10-29-isa-09-evidence.md:262:NS §0 promises “a named fault outcome … never a silent wrong answer”; §4.2 names distinct causes. ISA §4.1 says `udiv` gives “CC trap; R fault record” and `trap` likewise; §10 requires launch faults to be “codes of §5.4.” §7.1 explicitly gives allocation/free codes and §17.14 gives unclean-free code 4, but the missing CC cause mapping lets implementations disagree. §4.8 currently confines `fault` to Q oracle/lproc rows. A `hostcall bennett.fault` can export a requested code before a trap, but does not define `bennett_result_fault`'s code and is CC-only. Thus it is not a full realisation.
docs/notes/2026-10-29-isa-09-evidence.md:481:## I1: checkpointed and pebbled oracle realisations
docs/notes/2026-10-29-isa-09-evidence.md:507:> An expansion library MAY specify a checkpoint/replay or pebbled realisation of §5.2. It MUST name every generated allocation, copy, forward evaluation, inverse evaluation and release in a deterministic order, including every repeated evaluation, with distinct generated ordinals and scratch identities. The schedule binds that library's version and hash; all replay events are executed and charged. The reference body, F_B and budget unit are unchanged. The library MUST supply an exact permutation equivalence argument with clean private scratch on the full domain D; interfaces and borrowed correlations obey §5.2/5.6.
docs/notes/2026-10-29-isa-09-evidence.md:509:> The frame descriptor reports the realised retained history and checkpoint wires from the bound expansion; B×frame_width describes the full-history reference expansion. The default remains the full-history ref library. T2 applies to that reference expansion; a checkpointed expansion establishes the same postcondition by its checked equivalence and its compute/uncompute construction.
docs/notes/2026-10-29-isa-09-evidence.md:569:**Verdict: already-realised — budget provenance belongs in dialect/driver documentation; rungs 2/4/7/8 need complete bounds, not a new ISA value.**
docs/notes/2026-10-29-isa-09-evidence.md:605:B tests idle at n=1,2,3,4, active pc=0→HALT and a fault→HALT, every old frame becoming clean on inverse. For n=1, active pc=0 writes encoded old_pc=1; idle pc=1 writes 0. Include a coherent superposition of these branches; amplitude comparison must agree, with no implicit relative phase. C compares all target/fault seeds and F_B to 0.8. Hash impact V if adopted (new reference realisation even with equal final F_B). An optimiser could already use this as a §5.7 transformation; making it the reference is an efficiency/readiness choice, not a rung blocker.
docs/notes/2026-10-29-isa-09-evidence.md:677:> Invocation contexts are enumerated from the root realisation's complete invocation tree, including invocations selected only by defaults, not from schedule entries. A context key is its concrete outermost-first invocation chain with resolved loop iteration and oracle-step indices and classical arguments. Enumerate distinct keys in bytewise order of their JCS encodings. A subject claim checks every matching context; its bound is their common upper bound. Wildcard selectors are resolved before this enumeration.
docs/notes/2026-10-29-isa-09-evidence.md:749:No new 0.9 stack semantics are proposed. A bound expansion can pack proved padding under §5.7/T6, with I1's realised-frame reporting if adopted. B/C compare logical snapshots and single-step inverses of differently sized recursive callees; E charges actual bits/live intervals, while reference stack exhaustion remains at R_f sums. No new fault code; `FAULT_BUDGET_DEPTH` still occurs on `sp+R_f>stack_limit`, not on packed-byte occupancy. Existing example hashes unchanged.

../bennett/docs/council/isa-09-k82-2026-11-01/round-1/raw-sol-1.txt:2261:| `qinplace` | `qinplace @g @ginv tk x (ins..) (mem..) (args..)`; written `qinplace @g @ginv tk x` when all three lists are empty (and only then) | **Well-formed** iff `@g` and `@ginv` are oracles whose `in` slots are x's width n followed by the widths of `ins` in order, whose `tgt` width is n, whose fault widths are equal (F), whose `mem` slots match the `mem` regions (role `in`, §3.3's matching rule) and whose `args` types are those of `args`; x, the `ins` wires and the effective control context are pairwise disjoint. **Sem**: `t = qalloc any n; f = qalloc any F; oracle_call @g tk (x, ins..) t f (mem..) (args..); qexch x t; oracle_call @ginv tk (x, ins..) t f (mem..) (args..); qfree t; qfree f`, so x ← g(x; ins, mem, args); these are its generated operations, in this order (§11). **Inverse-pair obligation**: under the tuple of `tk`, the call's `args` values and the tuples of the tokens among them, on every basis value of x, of the `ins` wires and of the `mem` cells, both oracles are fault-free and g(·; ins, mem, args) and ginv(·; ins, mem, args) are mutually inverse permutations of the n-bit encoding. It is carried by an `inverse_pairs` entry (§12.3) of the certificate record of the region whose body contains the `qinplace`; an entry `{g: G, ginv: H}` covers both `qinplace @G @H` and `qinplace @H @G` (its claim checks both compositions). An entry **covers** a `qinplace` when its `budget` is componentwise ≤ the tuple of `tk` (success at a tuple implies the same result at every larger one, §5.4), every token-typed argument's tuple equals the entry's `arg_tokens` tuple for that argument (equality: a nested oracle's outcome may change with its budget), and every other `args` value lies in its claim's `args` domain. Its claim is an `exhaustive` claim with `expected: "inverse_pair"`, which the executor verifies as a checker does (once per closure hash, before first use), or a `structural` `inverse_pair_library` claim, which covers when the named claim of the linked library's certificate is such a claim for the same closure hashes of g and ginv (no re-execution; a library claim without an `args` domain covers every value; its proof or evidence label is the library claim's); a `sampled` claim never covers. Before any effect of a CC/R region call, every `qinplace` the callee executes (transitively; its token and `args` are resolved from the call's classical arguments, which reach lifted calls only as classical arguments, §4.2) MUST have a covering entry, else CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP`. The private t and f are not values of the region for §6: the certified pair cleans them, the only cleanup that is not a §6.4 realisation. Under control the allocations are unconditional and every gate of both calls and of the exchange is controlled |

# Checker question 1 — realised native operators

Proposal for council review; source request 1 in
`../bennett-conformance/checker/.tmp/CROSS_REPO_BEADS.md`.

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA §8.3's native `gates` entries contain opcode, classes, controls,
parameters, cost and epsilon; §8.1 supplies arithmetic expressions, not
an operator description. §12.1 requires the checker to build realised U
or a channel independently for numerical/channel claims, and §4.4
defines only the ideal opcode plus an error obligation. Cost and an
advertised distance bound do not identify the realised operation.
This is a genuine S0 contract gap for independent native approximation
verification (rubric C3/C5), not grounds to accept reference self-comparison.

North Star §§4.4/5.1 part 2 promises independent phase-sensitive and
channel verification. Returning unchecked for an unsupported model is
honest checker evidence handling, but is not the missing model mechanism.
Exact logical native gates retain their declared §4.4 matrices; algorithms
with nonzero approximation remain in scope.

## Alternatives and worked witness

Take one native X entry with fixed advertised epsilon=2 and time=1.
Realisation A is X and realisation B is −X. Both meet that advertised
operator-norm bound, cost, opcode and class metadata; they produce the
same uncontrolled channel. Their controlled extensions differ: on
`|+>_q|0>_t` they produce `(00+11)/sqrt(2)` and `(00−11)/sqrt(2)`,
orthogonal states. An entry with no realisation bytes cannot tell the
checker which process to compare, even though both targets have the same
current JSON/hash. Certifying actual bound 0 under coherent control is
valid only for A. A conservative declared bound 2 can be composed as an
assumption, but that is not independent reconstruction of a tighter claim.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Treat every native entry as its ideal opcode | Circular self-comparison for approximate entries. |
| Trust advertised epsilon | Explicit target assumption only; cannot discharge §12.1 independent reconstruction. |
| Hash-bound matrix/channel model (recommended) | Supplies the actual operation, controls and conventions needed for checking. |
| Model approximations as explicit existing schemes | Already works when every leaf is exact and the scheme lists the actual circuit; cannot describe an opaque approximate native leaf alone. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Add to §8.3 gate entries `realisation_model?: {version,kind,arity,basis,
parameter_bindings,control_models,data}` and append:

```text
A native entry with nonzero or expression-valued epsilon MUST supply a
versioned realisation_model. Its canonical bytes are part of target_hash.
kind is unitary or channel. basis lists ordered operand bits; matrices use
the §12.1 computational basis with inherited controls first. Parameters
bind by their exact §4.4 angle values and declared operand order. data
contains the actual complex matrix, or an ordered Kraus list, as exact
expressions over rationals, i, pi, sqrt, exp, sin and cos, or certified
interval enclosures of an identified exact model. Real/imaginary rational
constants are numerator/positive-denominator decimal-string pairs.
Intervals are closed rational endpoints; enclosures need an independently
checkable certificate for the enclosed exact operation and its unitary
or completely-positive trace-preserving property. An unverified box of
numbers alone is not an operation model.

For each admitted total control count, control_models binds the actual
controlled implementation, including environment/measurement conventions.
A unitary model may use the exact controlled-U construction including its
global phase. A channel is not given a coherent controlled extension by
controlling arbitrary Kraus operators; it requires an explicit instrument
or dilation/control model. The checker resolves every parameter and
control context and constructs the realised operation from that model,
checks dimensions and normalization, and verifies the claimed distance
with §12.1's interval algorithm. The epsilon field remains a separate
accuracy/matching obligation and is not the operator definition.
Exact entries may omit the model and then denote §4.4's exact opcode,
including its exact controlled form. A supplied model must agree with
that contract. No absent model licenses ideal-vs-ideal self-comparison.
```

For legacy targets lacking a model, preserve parse/execution compatibility
but record affected numerical/channel obligations as unchecked (missing
realisation model). Do not issue a checked executable certificate from
those missing obligations. The new model version is an explicit target
format extension; unknown model versions receive unsupported-model
reporting, never a guessed matrix.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

New native-model bytes change target_hash and every target-bound schedule
and certificate. No canonical source or reference catalog changes. Target
authors, scheduler and checker must resolve the same model and controlled
variant; a simulator using ideal opcodes alone is not a physical native
model checker. Physical stochastic failure probabilities remain separate
from logical approximation epsilon (see fd0).

## Conformance vectors and verification

Use X/−X with an outer control and compare matrix units/Choi operators;
test uncontrolled equal channels but unequal controlled processes. Exact
X without a model remains valid. Missing approximate model, wrong arity,
non-normalized Kraus list, parameter/control mismatch, a model-hash change
and a rigorously inconclusive interval claim must not pass the obligation.
The Bell-state sign witness is checked in
[contract-verification](2026-11-02-contract-verification.md). No native
target checker was executed for this note.

## Release and after acceptance

Recommend a **0.9 K contract repair with a versioned model schema**, or a
0.10 target extension with this current gap explicitly blocking independent
approximation claims. The checker question is already the cross-repo bead
filing request; the orchestrator should file/retain it in this repo with
this exact missing mechanism. Council must accept the model and migration
rules before a consumer advertises checked approximate native support.

## Exact arithmetic and document check

Run this extraction from the repository root under the environment prefix:

```sh
python3 -I -B - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path('docs/notes/2026-11-02-contract-verification.md')
source = p.read_text().split('```python\n', 1)[1].split('\n```', 1)[0]
exec(compile(source, str(p), 'exec'))
PY
```

The arithmetic is in Q[z]/(z^4+1), z=exp(i*pi/4), with rational
coefficients; H uses (z−z³)/2. There are no floating-point quantum
calculations in this check. Equality of every branch operator to V/sqrt(2)
establishes the resulting channel on all matrix units, hence also with
an external reference; the negative vectors compare density matrices.
This is exact finite mathematical verification, not a producer certificate
accepted by an implementation of the proposed contract.

```python
from fractions import Fraction as F
from pathlib import Path
from math import gcd
import hashlib
import re

def scalar(n):
    return (F(n), F(0), F(0), F(0))

ZERO, ONE = scalar(0), scalar(1)
Z = (F(0), F(1), F(0), F(0))

def add(a, b):
    return tuple(x + y for x, y in zip(a, b))

def neg(a):
    return tuple(-x for x in a)

def mul(a, b):
    out = [F(0)] * 7
    for i in range(4):
        for j in range(4):
            out[i+j] += a[i] * b[j]
    for k in range(6, 3, -1):
        out[k-4] -= out[k]
    return tuple(out[:4])

def conj(a):
    out = [a[0], F(0), F(0), F(0)]
    for k in range(1, 4):
        out[4-k] -= a[k]
    return tuple(out)

I = mul(Z, Z)
TINV = conj(Z)
HFACTOR = (F(0), F(1, 2), F(0), F(-1, 2))
assert mul(HFACTOR, HFACTOR) == scalar(F(1, 2))
assert mul(Z, TINV) == ONE
assert mul(I, I) == neg(ONE)

def compact(state):
    return {i: a for i, a in state.items() if a != ZERO}

def accumulate(state, i, a):
    state[i] = add(state.get(i, ZERO), a)

def enabled(i, controls):
    return all((i >> c) & 1 for c in controls)

def hadamard(state, wire, controls=()):
    out = {}
    mask = 1 << wire
    for i, a in state.items():
        if not enabled(i, controls):
            accumulate(out, i, a)
            continue
        a = mul(a, HFACTOR)
        low = i & ~mask
        accumulate(out, low, a)
        accumulate(out, low | mask, neg(a) if i & mask else a)
    return compact(out)

def flip(state, target, controls=()):
    out = {}
    for i, a in state.items():
        j = i ^ (1 << target) if enabled(i, controls) else i
        accumulate(out, j, a)
    return compact(out)

def phase(state, target, factor, controls=()):
    return compact({i: mul(a, factor) if (i >> target) & 1
                    and enabled(i, controls) else a
                    for i, a in state.items()})

# Bit positions: a=0, b=1, g=2, y=3, outer q=4.
C = [('h', 2), ('t', 2), ('cx', (0, 2)), ('tdg', 2),
     ('cx', (1, 2)), ('t', 2), ('cx', (0, 2)),
     ('tdg', 2), ('h', 2), ('sdg', 2)]
adj = {'h': 'h', 'cx': 'cx', 't': 'tdg', 'tdg': 't',
       's': 'sdg', 'sdg': 's'}
CDAG = [(adj[op], args) for op, args in reversed(C)]

def sequence(state, gates, controls=()):
    for op, args in gates:
        if op == 'h':
            state = hadamard(state, args, controls)
        elif op == 'cx':
            state = flip(state, args[1], (args[0],) + controls)
        else:
            factor = {'t': Z, 'tdg': TINV, 's': I, 'sdg': neg(I)}[op]
            state = phase(state, args, factor, controls)
    return state

for a in range(2):
    for b in range(2):
        i = a | (b << 1)
        assert sequence({i: ONE}, C) == {i | ((a*b) << 2): ONE}
        dirty_phase = I if (a, b) == (0, 1) else neg(I)
        assert sequence({i | 4: ONE}, C) == {
            i | ((1 ^ (a*b)) << 2): dirty_phase}

def expected(state, controlled, closed):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    state = flip(state, 3, (0, 1) + outer)
    return hadamard(state, 0, outer) if closed else state

def branches(state, controlled=False, closed=False, correction='correct'):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    state = sequence(state, C, outer)
    state = flip(state, 3, (2,) + outer)
    if closed:
        state = hadamard(state, 0, outer)
    state = hadamard(state, 2)  # Unconditional X-basis measurement.
    result = []
    for m in range(2):
        branch = {i: a for i, a in state.items() if ((i >> 2) & 1) == m}
        if m:
            branch = flip(branch, 2)  # Reset measured private wire.
            transport = closed and correction != 'stale'
            if transport:
                branch = hadamard(branch, 0, outer)
            if correction != 'none':
                controls = (0, 1) + (() if correction == 'uncontrolled' else outer)
                branch = {i: neg(a) if enabled(i, controls) else a
                          for i, a in branch.items()}
            if transport:
                branch = hadamard(branch, 0, outer)
        result.append(compact(branch))
    return result

branch_columns = 0
for controlled in (False, True):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    for i in range(32):
        if i & 4:
            continue
        start = {i: ONE}
        unitary = sequence(start, C, outer)
        unitary = flip(unitary, 3, (2,) + outer)
        unitary = sequence(unitary, CDAG, outer)
        assert unitary == expected(start, controlled, False)
        for closed in (False, True):
            want = {j: mul(a, HFACTOR)
                    for j, a in expected(start, controlled, closed).items()}
            for branch in branches(start, controlled, closed):
                assert branch == compact(want)
                branch_columns += 1
assert branch_columns == 128

def density(states):
    out = {}
    for state in states:
        for i, a in state.items():
            for j, b in state.items():
                accumulate(out, (i, j), mul(a, conj(b)))
    return compact(out)

superposition = {0: ONE}
for wire in (0, 1, 4):
    superposition = hadamard(superposition, wire)
for controlled, closed, correction in [
        (False, False, 'none'), (True, False, 'uncontrolled'),
        (False, True, 'stale')]:
    actual = density(branches(superposition, controlled, closed, correction))
    ideal = density([expected(superposition, controlled, closed)])
    assert actual != ideal, correction

# Imported model, unit conversion, sequence/parallel accounting.
def model(d):
    return 3*2*d, 2*d*d, F(1, 10) * F(1, 100)**((d+1)//2)
assert model(5) == (30, 50, F(1, 10**7))
assert model(3) == (18, 18, F(1, 100000))
assert F(30, 10**9) == F(3, 10**8)
assert 3*30*50 == 4500
assert (3*30, 50) == (90, 50)  # Sequential time/peak.
assert (30, 3*50) == (30, 150)  # Parallel time/peak.

# Width dispatch, persistent versus reversed load state.
assert (1 ^ 0, 5 ^ 3) == (1, 6)
def later_run(reverse):
    memory, bump = 7, 17  # A stored 7 and allocated at 16.
    if reverse:
        memory, bump = 0, 16
    return memory, bump
assert later_run(False) == (7, 17)
assert later_run(True) == (0, 16)

# Fixed-image coverage; hashing raw bytes differs from hashing hex text.
image_hash = hashlib.sha256(bytes([1, 2])).hexdigest()
assert image_hash != hashlib.sha256(b'0102').hexdigest()
assert 2**(1+1+4) == 64
assert F(64, 2**22) == F(1, 65536)
assert (2**16)*8 == 524288

# Exact modular orbit; no enormous enumeration.
for seed in (0, 3, 4):
    visited, current = set(), seed
    while current not in visited:
        visited.add(current)
        current = (current + 8) % 256
    assert visited == {x for x in range(256) if (x-seed) % 8 == 0}
    assert len(visited) == 32
assert (2**64)//gcd(8, 2**64) == 2**61

# Controlled X and -X produce orthogonal Bell states.
bell_plus = {0: HFACTOR, 3: HFACTOR}
bell_minus = {0: HFACTOR, 3: neg(HFACTOR)}
inner = ZERO
for i, a in bell_plus.items():
    inner = add(inner, mul(conj(a), bell_minus[i]))
assert inner == ZERO

# Fault counts concern code(F_B), not the incoming XOR word.
assert 4*4*16 == 256
assert 1 ^ 1 == 0  # Code 1 failure can leave final fault word zero.
assert 1 ^ 0 == 1  # Successful invocation can leave it nonzero.
assert (1+1, 2*2, max(1, 2)) == (2, 4, 2)  # Root time/occupancy/peak.
assert 1+2 != 2  # Child peaks cannot be summed to get root peak.

# Owned deliverable structure and local links.
base = Path('docs/notes')
ids = ['z4c', 'l2p', 'fd0', 'lpe', 'adg', 'xv4', 'b5b', '5u3',
       'checker-native-model', 'checker-diagnostics', 'checker-fault-rate',
       'checker-resource-contexts']
for ident in ids:
    path = base / ('2026-11-02-contract-' + ident + '.md')
    body = path.read_text()
    for word in ['Defect', 'North Star', 'Alternatives',
                 'Recommended exact spec diff', 'Hashes',
                 'Conformance', 'Release']:
        assert word.lower() in body.lower(), (ident, word)
    assert not re.search(r'\b(?:TODO|TBD)\b', body)
    assert sum(line.startswith('```') for line in body.splitlines()) % 2 == 0
    for target in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', body):
        if not target.startswith(('https://', 'http://', '#')):
            assert (path.parent / target.split('#')[0]).exists(), (ident, target)
print('PASS: 12 proposals; exact AND 8 columns/32 inverse columns; '
      '128 corrected branch columns; 3 negative channels; '
      'model/domain/orbit/resource arithmetic; fences and local links')
print('Fixed-image SHA-256:', image_hash)
```
