**Suite H / §11.1 K77 adapter vector.** Captured `oracle_invocation`
follows the source call's arguments and body/template capture, irrespective
of unrelated apply-site context; nested calls restore the enclosing context
on exit. K77 conformance includes the same §16.3 `inc8` closure and
positional vector `[x,t,f]` captured with B=2 and B=3, forwarded through
`invoke`/`call` and captured body/template adapters under a conflicting
apply-site context. Nested calls use their own explicit tuples. Assert
frame counts 2 and 3, final `(t,f)=(x+1 mod 256,1)` from zero seeds,
exact inverse restoration, and retained forward `call_index` values during
inverse traversal (0/1 for the two calls within one source-row expansion;
separate source points each start at 0). Reject absent, malformed or
source-inconsistent records before concrete expansion. This vector tests
the existing K77 binding rule; it adds no catalog argument or binding rule.

## 16. Canonical textual form (normative)
```

Ranges: null [0, 16), heap [16, 4096), both within [0, 2^12); no globals. For n = 3:
`q` is a fresh register R (width 3, |000⟩); `p` = 16 (the bump pointer
starts at `heap_base`); `q2` is an alias of R; iterations i = 0, 1, 2
each slice [i, 1) (a `qreg[1]` view) and flip that wire; at i = 3 the
loop exits; `measure.i3` finds width 3 and outcome 0b111 = 7 with
probability 1. The run is `COMPLETED` with one `INT` result of width 3
(the byte `07`) and one measurement record (R, bits 1, 1, 1); it is not
reversible (a `measure` frame). For n = 2 the loop flips two wires and
`measure.i3` traps before measuring (width 2 ≠ 3): `FAILED`, one record
`FAULT_UB_TRAP`, no measurement, R live in |11⟩; that run *is*
reverse-eligible and reversal returns R to |00⟩ and releases it. For n =
0 the loop does not run and `measure.i3` traps likewise.

**Example 5** (`scratch`, BLK-4): quantum allocation inside an R
function, used and released, then reversed.

```
bennett-object isa=0.9 object=0.9 launch=0.9 sched=0.9 cert=0.9 coverage=0.9 target=0.9 ptr_width=32

section symbols
{"0":{"interface_hash":"5cebd5a72ee7873edda0d2c35afed2a5ab79eba60f2b242b2a856d5fa4074634","kind":"cc","name":"main","signature":{"abi":"0.9","kind":"cc","params":["i64"],"results":[],"tier":"cc"}},"1":{"interface_hash":"05525834cbe5a518aa97444912254f3c12c75dd86314a98ba550f45187208a0b","kind":"r","name":"scratch","signature":{"abi":"0.9","kind":"r","params":["i64"],"results":[],"tier":"r"}},"2":{"interface_hash":"3860ea712b2d70fc495834586bb397656a467c85235aa069768318a06e9fa89b","kind":"perm_action","name":"flip","signature":{"abi":"0.9","args":[],"bounds":[],"in":[],"kind":"perm_action","mem":[],"mut":[1],"tier":"q"}}}

section functions
fn cc @main (n:i64) () () () ()
^entry():
call @scratch n
ret
fn r @scratch (n:i64) () () () ()
^entry():
q = qreg.new any n
zero = const.i64 0
one = const.i64 1
br ^test(zero)
^test(i:i64):
c = icmp.ult i n
br c ^body(i) ^done()
^body(j:i64):
s = qreg.slice q j 1
action_call @flip (s) () () ()
t = qreg.slice q j 1
action_call @flip (t) () () ()
j2 = add j one
br ^test(j2)
^done():
qreg.free q
ret
perm_action @flip (a:wire) () () () ()
^entry():
x a
```

The object is memoryless. Forward, for every n the device can provide (else `qreg.new` records `FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP`): R creates register Q
(width n, |0…0⟩); iteration j flips wire j to |1⟩ and back to |0⟩; the
`qreg.free` finds Q clean (a verifying executor verifies it); the run is
`COMPLETED` with no values. Whole-run reversal re-creates Q clean with
its id, undoes iteration n − 1 (the second flip's adjoint makes wire n −
1 |1⟩, its slice frame restores Q live, the first flip's adjoint makes
it |0⟩), then every earlier iteration, and finally the `qreg.new` (Q is
clean: released); the run is `REVERSED` with the register ledger as at
entry, Q's id not reused. On hardware the free is unverified (§17 item
14) and `bennett_reverse` returns `E_NOT_REVERSIBLE`. Under 0.8 `@scratch`
is ill-formed (`qreg.*` was CC only).

**Example 6** (`adaptive`, BLK-2): a budget minted on the device from a
run-time value.

```
bennett-object isa=0.9 object=0.9 launch=0.9 sched=0.9 cert=0.9 coverage=0.9 target=0.9 ptr_width=32

section symbols
{"0":{"interface_hash":"b80aed1639a9ac6a6856f02b0b3264d905e4eea3fa3f9081c01e56283ea398cf","kind":"cc","name":"adaptive","signature":{"abi":"0.9","kind":"cc","params":["i1","qreg[8]","qreg[8]","qreg[4]"],"results":[],"tier":"cc"}},"1":{"interface_hash":"c0d9835844fa882c1864044acc6fa26dba3935e0d6a9d33f9cdbf1e7c877d250","kind":"cc","name":"overdraw","signature":{"abi":"0.9","kind":"cc","params":[],"results":[],"tier":"cc"}},"2":{"interface_hash":"ed9865300b85a519c2379de98e36e635e72b5b78d8bdfca03dccf9a09aa3df5e","kind":"oracle","name":"inc8","signature":{"abi":"0.9","args":[],"fault":4,"in":[8],"kind":"oracle","mem":[],"tgt":8,"tier":"q"}}}

section functions
fn cc @adaptive (b:i1, x:qreg[8], t:qreg[8], f:qreg[4]) () () () ()
^entry():
two = const.i64 2
three = const.i64 3
zero = const.i64 0
s = select b three two
tk = budget.mint @inc8 s zero zero zero
oracle_call @inc8 tk (x) t f () ()
ret
fn cc @overdraw () () () () ()
^entry():

exec
/bin/bash -lc "sed -n '1,100p' docs/notes/2026-11-02-contract-verification.md && sed -n '290,360p' docs/notes/2026-11-02-contract-verification.md" in /home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec

# fd0 — imported QEC models, parameters and hierarchy

Proposal for council review; `b-isa-spec-fd0`, originating at
`b-targets-gyp`. No published target or spec bytes are edited.

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §8.3 imports maps a model quantity to `[resource,factor]`.
§8.1 has a closed expression language and binds no code distance,
hardware parameter or imported quantity. §8.2 aggregates scheduled leaf
events, not a factory hierarchy or a symbolic algorithm call graph.
There is no binding rule from an imported QEC formula to a logical gate's
time or qubit class. A numeric mapping is implemented by the supplied
bead's account; it does not supply these missing semantics.

North Star §7, **Qualtran, Azure RE**, promises symbolic hierarchical
counting and imported models as target data. §4.3 requires all declared
resources with their own units. This is an S0 contract scope gap for that
import capability, rather than permission to reject such algorithms.
Scalar materialisation is an available 0.9 path for a completely bound
instance; it is not the full symbolic contract. Keep fd0 as the explicit
blocker for that missing contract, even if the new encoding is 0.10.

Primary evidence: Microsoft's [QEC architecture model example](https://quantum.microsoft.com/en-us/insights/blogs/resource-estimation/modeling-quantum-architecture-with-azure-quantum-resource-estimator)
uses code distance and hardware timing in its cycle formula. Qualtran's
own [call graph protocol](https://qualtran.readthedocs.io/en/latest/resource_counting/call_graph.html)
admits symbolic multiplicities. Its [release notes](https://github.com/quantumlib/Qualtran/releases)
say AlgorithmSummary was removed in favor of GateCounts; adapters must pin
the input format/version, not assume the old class is current. These
sources were read as model-format evidence, not imported code.

## Alternatives and worked example

Use a declared odd code distance d=5, hardware measurement time τ=2 ns,
and **illustrative** formulas chosen for this witness:
`cycle=3τd`, `physical=2d²`, `logical_risk=(1/10)*(1/100)^((d+1)/2)`.
The results are 30 ns, 50 physical qubits per logical qubit and 1/10^7.
The first expression can be numerically precomputed; it cannot reference
d in current §8.1. The risk expression needs exponentiation. It is a
physical-model risk, not a bound on phase-sensitive operator norm.

For three sequential cycles with one logical qubit, the model gives
90 ns, peak 50 physical qubits and occupancy 4500 physical-qubit·ns.
For three simultaneous independent logical qubits held for one cycle,
time is 30 ns and peak is 150, with the same occupancy. Adding all scalar
quantities would get the parallel time or sequential peak wrong.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Bind d and hardware externally; emit a scalar 0.9 target | Sound concrete compatibility path; retain the original model and bindings in provenance. No symbolic selection or hierarchy is represented in the target. |
| Add parameters and formulas only | Solves evaluation, but not where a formula is consumed, hierarchical multiplicities, concurrency or double counting. Insufficient alone. |
| Bound target model plus separate hierarchical estimate (recommended) | Fix hardware/model choices in target identity; bind symbolic algorithm inputs and model accounting to the certificate; retain §8.2 leaf accounting separately. |
| Treat failure probability as gate epsilon or sum every quantity | Unsound error semantics or wrong units/aggregation. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

For the extended target/cert version, add §8.5 and a corresponding K entry:

```text
8.5 Imported model bindings
A target MAY contain model_parameters:{name:{value:number|null,unit:string}}
and each imports entry MAY additionally contain
{source_hash, source_format, parameters:{name:expr},
 quantities:{name:{unit,expr}}, bindings:{destination:quantity_name}}.
source_hash is SHA-256 of the source model's canonical bytes; source_format
includes a format version. References {param:name} resolve model_parameters;
{quantity:name} resolves a quantity of this model. Dependencies form a DAG;
cycles, duplicate definitions and undeclared references are invalid.
Formula expressions add pow(base,exponent) for integer exponents and
sqrt(x) for the nonnegative principal root, in model context only.
Rational operations and integer powers are exact; roots are exact
algebraic quantities, converted to a reported decimal only with a stated
outward bound. Division by zero, negative roots and nonfinite concrete
outputs are errors. No eps or accuracy-bucket variable is bound here.
Integer parameters declare their integer constraint; a code-distance
parameter additionally declares its permitted parity/range.
A null parameter is symbolic: an estimate retains it as a free parameter.
It MUST be bound before concrete target scheduling or numeric evaluation,
and every concrete binding is included in the resolved target's bytes.
Only an explicit bindings entry may substitute a model quantity for a
gate cost, class quantity or other target numeric field. Its destination
must name a schema-defined numeric field and have matching units and a
valid value. Do not infer bindings from vendor quantity names.
Unmatched quantities are preserved under new named resources using map
and its unit factor. An imported physical failure-risk quantity is a
separately named model output or additive count charge, never epsilon,
an operator-norm assertion, or an automatic probability composition rule.
```

Add to §12.3 region records:

```text
model_estimates?:[{model_hash,parameters,root, nodes,outputs,verification}]
```

And add this normative paragraph:

```text
A model estimate is a finite DAG of leaf model invocations and composition
nodes {kind:sequence|parallel|repeat,children,count?}. A repeat count is a
nonnegative integer expression. Children have explicit resource units,
duration and live-interval summaries. Sequence shifts each child's time
origin to its predecessor's end; parallel aligns child origins; repeat
concatenates count copies. Count resources sum, time is makespan, and
physical occupancy and peak are computed from the resulting simultaneous
live intervals, summing simultaneous demands. Shared factories require
explicit shared intervals, not duplicated independent children. Symbolic
results retain their DAG until parameters are bound. These reports are
distinct from required §8.2 resources and MUST NOT be added to the same
leaf counts again. The checker verifies model identity, parameter and unit
bindings, multiplicities and composition, recording checked obligations
separately from external-model assumptions. Unsupported symbolic source
expressions remain preserved external model obligations; they do not
invalidate execution of the object or justify an invented numerical
estimate. A concrete requested estimate with an unresolved obligation is
reported unchecked, with the exact expression and missing model support.
```

The external obligation path preserves the full imported artifact and
symbolic report; it does not claim a checked estimate. Concrete adapter
support remains required work under fd0. Code-distance search belongs to
the estimator/scheduler; its selected value is a resolved target binding,
and its search domain/objective/physical assumptions belong in the model
estimate. The target does not silently choose an optimum.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

Resolved parameters, formulas, input-model identity and bindings change
target_hash. Algorithm-dependent hierarchy changes certificate bytes,
not source closure hashes. No reference catalog leaf needs to change.
Targets, estimator, scheduler and checker must agree on units, exact
evaluation, outward conversion and DAG accounting. Existing count
resources and exact logical-gate epsilon keep their present meanings.
Do not reinterpret a legacy count named failure_probability as a checked
physical-error model.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Check the d=5 example above using rational arithmetic; d=3 gives cycle
18 ns, physical 18 and risk 1/100000. A seconds conversion multiplies
nanoseconds by 1/10^9. Exercise null/bound parameters, odd-distance
validation, forward quantity references, cycles, zero divisors, roots,
incompatible units, unknown binding destinations and a source-hash
mismatch. Test sequence versus parallel peaks, factory sharing and
symbolic repeats. A nonzero risk with exact logical gate epsilon=0 must
leave the logical error bound at zero. The executable arithmetic checks
are in [contract-verification](2026-11-02-contract-verification.md);
no target consumer was executed here.

## Release and after acceptance

Recommend **0.10 target/certificate extension**, with a 0.9 K entry
explicitly recording the blocker and the scalar-instance compatibility
construction. The fd0 proposal in the bead is incomplete without binding
destinations, hierarchy and identity. Do not call imported symbolic models
implemented until those vectors and consumer obligations pass. Council
acceptance precedes schema edits, adapter work and any re-pinning.

## Exact arithmetic and document check

Run this extraction from the repository root under the environment prefix:

```sh
python3 -I -B - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path('docs/notes/2026-11-02-contract-verification.md')
source = p.read_text().split('```python\n', 1)[1].split('\n```', 1)[0]

```

The arithmetic is in Q[z]/(z^4+1), z=exp(i*pi/4), with rational
coefficients; H uses (z−z³)/2. There are no floating-point quantum
calculations in this check. Equality of every branch operator to V/sqrt(2)
establishes the resulting channel on all matrix units, hence also with
an external reference; the negative vectors compare density matrices.
This is exact finite mathematical verification, not a producer certificate
accepted by an implementation of the proposed contract.
# Fixed-image coverage; hashing raw bytes differs from hashing hex text.
image_hash = hashlib.sha256(bytes([1, 2])).hexdigest()
assert image_hash != hashlib.sha256(b'0102').hexdigest()
assert 2**(1+1+4) == 64
assert F(64, 2**22) == F(1, 65536)
assert (2**16)*8 == 524288

# Exact modular orbit; no enormous enumeration.
for seed in (0, 3, 4):
    visited, current = set(), seed
    while current not in visited:
        visited.add(current)
        current = (current + 8) % 256
    assert visited == {x for x in range(256) if (x-seed) % 8 == 0}
    assert len(visited) == 32
assert (2**64)//gcd(8, 2**64) == 2**61

# Controlled X and -X produce orthogonal Bell states.
bell_plus = {0: HFACTOR, 3: HFACTOR}
bell_minus = {0: HFACTOR, 3: neg(HFACTOR)}
inner = ZERO
for i, a in bell_plus.items():
    inner = add(inner, mul(conj(a), bell_minus[i]))
assert inner == ZERO

# Fault counts concern code(F_B), not the incoming XOR word.
assert 4*4*16 == 256
assert 1 ^ 1 == 0  # Code 1 failure can leave final fault word zero.
assert 1 ^ 0 == 1  # Successful invocation can leave it nonzero.
assert (1+1, 2*2, max(1, 2)) == (2, 4, 2)  # Root time/occupancy/peak.
assert 1+2 != 2  # Child peaks cannot be summed to get root peak.

# Owned deliverable structure and local links.
base = Path('docs/notes')
ids = ['z4c', 'l2p', 'fd0', 'lpe', 'adg', 'xv4', 'b5b', '5u3',
       'checker-native-model', 'checker-diagnostics', 'checker-fault-rate',
       'checker-resource-contexts']
for ident in ids:
    path = base / ('2026-11-02-contract-' + ident + '.md')
    body = path.read_text()
    for word in ['Defect', 'North Star', 'Alternatives',
                 'Recommended exact spec diff', 'Hashes',
                 'Conformance', 'Release']:
        assert word.lower() in body.lower(), (ident, word)
    assert not re.search(r'\b(?:TODO|TBD)\b', body)
    assert sum(line.startswith('```') for line in body.splitlines()) % 2 == 0
    for target in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', body):
        if not target.startswith(('https://', 'http://', '#')):
            assert (path.parent / target.split('#')[0]).exists(), (ident, target)
print('PASS: 12 proposals; exact AND 8 columns/32 inverse columns; '
      '128 corrected branch columns; 3 negative channels; '
      'model/domain/orbit/resource arithmetic; fences and local links')
print('Fixed-image SHA-256:', image_hash)
```

## Verification limits

The exact checks establish the finite witnesses and internal document
consistency. They do not prove the proposed general contract sound in
all cases, accept a new K entry, close an implementation bead, or show
that SQLite's full reference analysis converges in practical time.
Each item states its own remaining acceptance vectors and consumer work.

## 9. Objects, identity, linking, reversible ABI (object 0.9)

### 9.1 Sections and schemas

Order fixed: `symbols imports functions data devices layout schedule
certificate coverage`; each at most once; unknown sections or fields are
errors. Metadata sections are one JCS line each, an object:

```
  symbols:  { "<local index>": { "name", "kind": "cc"|"r"|"oracle"|"action"|"perm_action"|"lproc"|"data", "signature": <sig>, "interface_hash" } }
  imports:  { "<name>": { "signature": <sig>, "pinned_closure_hash"?: hex } }
  devices:  { "models": { "<dev>": { "version": "0.9" } }, "services": { "<reverse-dns id>": { "version": "…" } } }
  layout:   { "ptr_width", "H", "A_tab", "S_cells", "null_size", "heap_base", "heap_limit_max",
              "globals": { "<name>": { "base", "size", "image": "<data name>" } },
              "interface_regions": { "<oracle>": { "<region>": { "base" } } }, "block_image"?: "<data name>" }
  schedule: §11      certificate: §12      coverage: { "entries": [ §13 ] }
  <sig>:    kind "oracle": { "tier":"q", "kind", "in":[w…], "tgt":w, "fault":F, "mem":[{"A","W","role"}], "args":[types], "abi":"0.9" }
            kind "action"/"perm_action": { "tier":"q", "kind", "mut":[w…], "in":[w…], "mem":[…], "args":[types], "bounds":[…], "abi" }
            kind "lproc": { "tier":"q", "kind", "params":[w…], "result":w, "tokens":["token"…], "args":[types], "mem":[{"A","W","role":"in"}], "abi" }
            kind "cc"/"r": { "tier", "kind", "params":[types], "results":[types], "abi" }
            kind "data": { "tier":"-", "kind", "size", "abi" }
```

Integers that may exceed 2^53 are decimal strings, and a JSON number ≥
2^53 anywhere in metadata is an error. `data` lines are `NAME SP HEX`.
**Encodings.** `types` in `args`, `params` and `results` are the §16.2
type spellings as JSON strings (`"i64"`, `"ptr"`, `"f64"`, `"token"`,
`"qreg[8]"`); `w` in `in`, `tgt`, `mut`, lproc `params` and `result` is
the integer bundle or location width (§16.3). An lproc's `tokens`,
`args` and `mem` arrays are mandatory (empty arrays when absent from the
header); `args` excludes tokens and `mem` has only `in` roles (§3.7).
Array order is header order within each group. A **program-point id**
(§9.2) is the JSON array `[interface_hash, block name, ordinal]`.
Certificate `frame` fields use exactly the typed-record schema of §9.4;
unknown fields are errors there as in every metadata record.
`devices.models` lists every §7.3 model that an `effect` of the object
uses (an `effect` on an unlisted §7.3 model is a load error; a device
outside §7.3 is never listed and faults at run time, §17 item 3; a key
that is not a §7.3 model is an unknown field); `devices.services` lists
every `hostcall` service (§10). An interface region's name (its param
name and its `layout.interface_regions.<oracle>` key) MUST differ from
every symbol and import name of the object, since `@name` in `lift.addr`
denotes either (§9.2).

### 9.2 Identity

An lproc's token, classical and region parameter groups are part of
its JCS signature (§9.1), including their order and types. The header
and every forwarded operand list are canonical function bytes; changing
them changes the envelope and, through references, the caller's closure
hash. Fixed binding instances are derived per oracle (§3.7), not new
symbols or separately hashed bodies; instance rows share the source
row's program-point id and expand once per fixed row instance per step,
while the instance and semantic-call occurrence components in §11
invocation paths and §12.1 context keys distinguish their uses. [deps: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-12.1-P11] Entry argument values and token tuples bind invocations through
`params_hash`, not through the lproc's interface hash (this section).
Imports must match all five groups; validation and instance formation
are repeated after linking (§9.3).

**Canonical function bytes**: the text from the function's header line
through its last instruction line, lines joined by LF, no trailing LF.
**Semantic envelope** of a symbol (byte-exact; every part ends with LF):

```
  (1) "isa=" <full version string, e.g. 0.9>
  (2) "ptr_width=" INT
  (3) the JCS signature
  (4) the canonical function bytes (with reference tokens replaced, below); for a data symbol: its data line NAME SP HEX
  (5a) for every data entry in the closure set, sorted by name bytewise:        "data " NAME " " HEX
  (5b) for every layout leaf in the closure set, sorted by JCS path bytes: "layout " <JCS key-component array> " " <JCS value>. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1]
  (5c) for an R/CC function, for every device model and service it uses, sorted: "device " <name> " " <JCS entry>
  (6) for every referenced symbol outside the symbol's SCC, in order of first reference: its 64-hex hash
```

**Closure set**, by symbol kind. **Oracle**: **all** `layout` leaves of
the object except `interface_regions` entries of other oracles (every
global's pre-entry affects its allocation faults), its own `interface_
regions.<oracle>.*` leaves, and every data image those leaves name.
**Lproc**: the leaves and images of every global `@g` it names in `lift.
addr`. **Action / permutation action**: no layout leaves; the `@image`
of each `qregion` is an ordinary symbol reference (replaced in part (4),
listed in part (6)). **CC / R function**: all `layout` leaves, every
data image they name, and in (5c) every device model and `hostcall`
service it uses. **Data symbol**: parts (5)–(6) are empty. A layout leaf
is a scalar or an object at the deepest level the schema lists (`globals.
<name>` and `interface_regions.<oracle>.<region>` are leaves; `ptr_
width`, `H`, … are leaves); a scalar's JCS value is its decimal text.
**Globals are data symbols**: every global `g` in `layout.globals` MUST
have `image` = `g` and the object MUST define a `data` symbol named `g`
of that size, so the token `@g` in `lift.addr` is both the global and a
symbol reference (replaced by the data symbol's hash; the global's
`base` and `size` enter through the layout leaf). An `@name` naming an
interface region (in `lift.addr`) is not a symbol reference and is left
unchanged. **Reference replacement**: in parts
(4)–(5) every `@name` symbol-reference token is replaced by `@` followed
by the referenced symbol's 64-hex hash (the symbol's own name in its
header line is a definition, not a reference, and is left unchanged); the **interface hash** uses
interface hashes of defined symbols and `SHA-256(name ‖ 0x00 ‖ JCS
signature)` (hex) for unresolved imports; the **closure hash** uses
closure hashes. **Acyclic and cyclic symbols.** The reference graph
has an edge from a symbol to every symbol it references (parts
(4)–(5)). An **acyclic** symbol — a one-member SCC that does not
reference itself — has hash = SHA-256 of its own envelope (example 1's
`inc8`, example 2's `zero` and `sum_db`); the SCC procedure below applies
to every **cyclic** SCC, including a one-member SCC whose function
references itself (example 2's `sum`). **SCCs**: members ordered by name bytewise; every intra-
SCC `@name` token (parts (4)–(5)) is replaced by `@$<decimal index>`;
intra-SCC references contribute no part-(6) line; `scc_hash` = SHA-256
over the concatenation of the members' envelopes in that order; member
hash = SHA-256 over the ASCII bytes of `<scc_hash hex>:<decimal index>`.
**object_hash** = SHA-256 of the canonical object text with the
`schedule`, `certificate` and `coverage` sections deleted together with
the blank line preceding each. `reference_program_hash` = the oracle's
interface hash. `params_hash` = SHA-256 of the JCS array of classical
parameter values (the typed encoding of §12.3, K13); `schedule_hash` =
SHA-256 of the schedule's JCS bytes.
**Program-point id** = `(symbol interface hash, block name, instruction
ordinal within the block counting nested lines in text order)`.
Executable certificates and caches bind to **closure hashes** (required,
§12).

A layout leaf's path is the JCS array of its original JSON key
components from the layout root to the deepest schema-listed leaf,
so a scalar has a one-component path and an interface-region leaf has
path `["interface_regions","<oracle>","<region>"]`.
[deps: ISA-9.1-P1, ISA-16.1]
Part (5b) emits exactly one line for each structured leaf in the closure
set, including distinct leaves whose dotted renderings coincide, ordered
by the unsigned UTF-8 bytes of that JCS path array, independently of the
leaf values; no dotted rendering is used as a key or an ordering key.
[deps: ISA-9.1-P1, ISA-16.1]
An oracle's own interface-region leaves and entries of other oracles
are selected by equality of the original JSON key under
`interface_regions` to the oracle's name, never by a prefix of a
rendered path; an lproc's global leaves are selected by equality of
the key under `globals` to the global name.
[deps: ISA-3.7-P1, ISA-9.1-P1]
call @work p
ret
fn r @work (p:ptr) () () () ()
^entry():
v = const.i64 7
store.i64 p v
effect clock.read p
fault 21

section devices
{"models":{"clock":{"version":"0.9"}},"services":{}}

section layout
{"A_tab":4,"H":12,"S_cells":0,"globals":{},"heap_base":16,"heap_limit_max":4080,"interface_regions":{},"null_size":16,"ptr_width":32}
```

`p` = 16; the store writes `07 00 00 00 00 00 00 00` at [16, 24); the clock
read writes c = 0 there (`00` × 8) and sets c = 1; `fault 21` ends the run:
`FAILED`, `bennett_result_nfaults` = 1, `bennett_result_fault` = 21, no
values, `@main`'s `ret` not executed, [16, 24) = `00` × 8, c = 1, tape
length 1. `bennett_reverse` (0.8: `E_NOT_REVERSIBLE`) removes the fault
record, undoes the clock read ([16, 24) = `07` then `00` × 7, c = 0, tape empty),
the store ([16, 24) = `00` × 8), the open activation of `@work` and the
call, and the allocation: the run is `REVERSED` with memory, allocation
table, clock and tape at their entry values.

**Example 9** (`zero_cause`, effective cause): a literal fault code 0.

```
bennett-object isa=0.9 object=0.9 launch=0.9 sched=0.9 cert=0.9 coverage=0.9 target=0.9 ptr_width=32

section symbols
{"0":{"interface_hash":"badf7dd2a59afa879f092921946539dfb918cedd35f6fbbaa838c630577eaed4","kind":"oracle","name":"zero_cause","signature":{"abi":"0.9","args":[],"fault":4,"in":[1],"kind":"oracle","mem":[],"tgt":1,"tier":"q"}},"1":{"interface_hash":"0403d76ff065acab57cb9165cfe1d3d73bbf8dad60bbc1348fb0305ee4f37611","kind":"cc","name":"zero_cc","signature":{"abi":"0.9","kind":"cc","params":[],"results":[],"tier":"cc"}}}

section functions
oracle @zero_cause (x:wire in) (t:wire) (f:wire[4]) () ()
^entry():
lift.faultif x 0
lift.retval x
fn cc @zero_cc () () () () ()
^entry():
fault 0
```

Rows: `lift.faultif` (index 0), `lift.retval` (1); `HALT` = 2, P = 2. With
B = 0 every x yields (0, `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS`). With B = 1, x = 0 yields (0,
`FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS`) (not halted) and x = 1 yields (0, `FAULT_UB_TRAP`).
With B ≥ 2, x = 0 yields (0, 0) (success, payload 0) and x = 1 yields (0,
`FAULT_UB_TRAP`); under 0.8, x = 1 yielded (0, 0) for every B ≥ 1,
indistinguishable from success. A run of `@zero_cc` is `FAILED` with one
record `FAULT_UB_TRAP`.

# spec/

`Bennett-ISA-Spec.md` is the normative text: draft 0.8, **accepted by the
three-model council on 2026-10-28 (round 8, all members accept, no blocking
issues, mean 4.17/5)**. Council records and the runner live in the `bennett`
meta repo (`docs/council/isa-2026-10-28/`, `tools/council.py`).

Rules for changing it:

- Every semantic change bumps the full version string (section 1); drafts
  are not envelope-compatible, so the four example hashes in section 16.3
  change with it. Run `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py --repatch`
  then `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py`.
- Surgical edits only; a rewrite has introduced a mechanical error every
  time it was tried.
- Add every new example or stated result to `isa_check.py` before anyone
  grades it.
- Section 17 lists decisions deferred to implementation with their
  defaults; an implementer who must choose something not covered there
  files an issue and the default goes into section 17 first.
- Revisions go back through the council.

### 5.7 Executor transformations

Inlining, structure recovery, dispatch re-indexing and simplification
MUST produce on every x ∈ D the same final interface tuple as §5.2's
construction on the reference body (T6). Checkpointed and pebbled
realisations: §17 item 18.

## 6. Uncomputation validity (static discipline)

### 6.1 Provenance and transfer

Every workspace bundle version, location version and cell version has
provenance `perm` (computed by permutation gates from its **dependency
set** S of versions) or `other`. Cell versions `(region, i, v)` advance on
every write. Π is the effective control context at the instruction.

| Instruction | Effect |
|---|---|
| `qalloc` | perm, S = ∅ |
| `d = qcopy s` | d: perm, S = {s} ∪ Π |
| `d = qload r a` | d: perm, S = {a} ∪ cells(r) ∪ Π |
| `qxor s t` | t: perm with S_t ∪ S_s ∪ Π if both perm, else other |
| `qexch a b` | each output: perm with S_a ∪ S_b ∪ Π if both perm, else other |
| `qstore r a v` | each cell (r,i): version advances; perm with S_cell ∪ S_v ∪ {a} ∪ Π if both perm, else other |
| `qexchm r a v` | v and every cell: perm with S_v ∪ ⋃S_cells ∪ {a} ∪ Π if all perm, else other; cell versions advance |
| permutation gate, controls C, target t | t: perm with S_t ∪ C ∪ ⋃S_c ∪ Π iff t was perm, else other |
| diagonal gate on u | unchanged |
| non-diagonal gate on u | u: other; closes the window of every value whose S contains u |
| `oracle_call @o (ins) tgt fr (mem)` | tgt, fr: perm with S = ins ∪ {tgt, fr} ∪ cells(every region passed) ∪ Π; `in` regions unchanged |
| `lift.perm_call` / permutation `action_call` | each mut: perm with S = mut ∪ ins ∪ cells(regions passed) ∪ Π; `mut`-region cells advance with the same S |
| non-permutation `action_call` | every mut and every `mut`-region cell: other |
| `qinplace @g @ginv tk x (ins) (mem) (args)` | x: perm with S_x ∪ ins ∪ cells(every region in `mem`) ∪ Π if x was perm, else other; the `ins` wires and `mem` cells are unchanged |
| lifted rows | each written location/cell: perm with the union of its read operands' sets ∪ Π; written cells' versions advance |
| `qregion` creation | every cell: version 0, perm, S = ∅, with the declared image as its initial value. [deps: ISA-4.6-P1] |

**Outstanding dependence** (for `other` cleanup) extends the dependency
test beyond workspace provenance sets to the joint state of all other
live values, including interface `mut` wires, region cells, borrowed
wires and outstanding diagonal phases. [deps: ISA-3.3-P3, ISA-5.1, ISA-5.3-P1, ISA-6.1-P1]
For a candidate inverse of the computing sequence C of g, let G be g's
wires and M the composed intervening operation after C and before that
inverse, retaining every intervening gate and its effective controls,
with internal clean and borrowed lifetimes interpreted as in §14.2.
[deps: ISA-2-P10, ISA-3.5, ISA-4, ISA-14.2]
The remaining values or phases **depend on g** iff there is no single
operator V on the remaining wires such that M = I_G ⊗ V for every state
of G and every input in the remaining wires' declared domain, including
arbitrary external entanglement and the exact global phase.
[deps: ISA-5.2-P4, ISA-5.3-P1, ISA-5.6-P1, ISA-14.2]
This is a joint, transitive test on the composed operation: cleaning or
releasing an intermediate value does not erase its already-transferred
dependence, and a diagonal use remains an outstanding phase unless its
inverse cancels it in M. [deps: ISA-4.4, ISA-6.3, ISA-14.2]
Dependency-set propagation through reads supplies such transitive paths,
but absence of a direct provenance edge to g alone does not establish
the required factorisation. [deps: ISA-6.1-P1, ISA-14.2]
A producer MAY establish non-dependence by the dependency-set test: if no
live value, interface wire, region cell or uncancelled diagonal phase has
a transitive read path from g in the intervening sequence, then M
factorises as I_G ⊗ V; failure of this test does not by itself prove
dependence.

### 6.2 Window and obligations

A perm value g with set S is **in its window** from its computation
until the first of: a non-diagonal gate on a wire in S or in g; a version
advance of a cell in S; a non-permutation action call touching a wire in
S; a permutation change to a wire in S not undone before g's cleanup.
A permutation change to a wire of S is **undone** when it and its
undoing form a matched pair (§5.3), and every intervening operation
leaves the computational-basis values of S and g unchanged; a cell
version advance still closes the window even if its value is restored.
[deps: ISA-5.3, ISA-6.1-P1, ISA-14.2]
Each cleanup is bound to its exact computing sequence and requires the
external input versions that sequence read (the versions in S for a
perm value), and the version of g it produced, to be current; g's
pre-computation target versions are not required to be current.
[deps: ISA-3.3-P3, ISA-6.1-P1]
A cleanup runs under the same effective control set, at the same wire
versions, as its computing sequence. [deps: ISA-2-P10, ISA-3.5, ISA-5.3-P5]
Every perm value MUST be
cleaned within its window and before its region exits; otherwise the
region is **ill-formed** (closed-window measurement-based cleanup is not
admitted). `other` values are released only by the adjoint of their
computing sequence, and only while no other live value, interface wire
or outstanding diagonal phase depends on them, as defined in §6.1.
[deps: ISA-5.3, ISA-6.1, ISA-6.4]

### 6.3 T5

Cleaning g within its window returns g's wires to |0⟩ and factorises
them from the remaining state; diagonal uses of g or of its dependencies
have applied the corresponding diagonal phases to the remainder, which
is part of the program's declared semantics.

### 6.4 Effective control and cleanup realisations

Every cleanup in this version is **unitary**: realisation `reverse`
(the adjoint of g's exact computing sequence, §6.2) or `recompute`
(legal for `perm` values only: recompute g into a fresh clean bundle by
the same sequence, `qxor` it into g's wires, uncompute the fresh bundle
by the adjoint). **Measurement-based cleanup is not admitted in sched/
cert 0.9**: a schedule naming any other realisation is invalid, the
target capability `mbu` is reserved and MUST be `false`, and no cleanup
produces a `channel` claim. *(non-normative)* The fixup after an X-basis
measurement of g with outcome m is the phase (−1)^{m·g(x)}, a function
of g's computing sequence and not of the target; its normative form,
its controlled variant and its certificate kind are deferred (§17 item
6; default: unitary cleanup). Schedules and certificate cleanup entries
are keyed by `(region hash, variant, inherited control count)`; a region
reachable under a non-empty control context MUST have a cleanup
schedule for that key.

both mappings refer to the same passed cells, with no byte copy.

For every x, a ∈ [0,4) and byte d ∈ [0,256), an outer tuple
(B ≥ 8, 0, stack_limit ≥ 8, depth_limit ≥ 1) and `tk` tuple (6,0,0,0)
give F_B(x,d) = (((x + a + (d mod 4)) mod 4) XOR 1, 0).
For arbitrary initial target t and fault f, the final values are
(t XOR payload, f); x and d are unchanged and all workspace is clean.
This permutation extends by linearity to superpositions and an
entangled reference. With outer B = 7 the success path has not executed
`lift.retval`, so the outcome is (0, `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS`); with outer
stack_limit = 7 or depth_limit = 0 the first row gives
(0, `FAULT_BUDGET_DEPTH`). With `tk` tuple (5,0,0,0), the nested oracle
returns fault 1; the lproc's `lift.faultif` propagates code 9 at outer
step 5, so B ≥ 5 gives (0, `FAULT_CALLEE`). The nested fault, stack
history, parameters and locations are cleared by prefix reversal; no
additional charge arises from forwarding, commit or reversal.

## 17. Decisions deferred to implementation, with defaults

Each item is one an implementer must decide to build; any decision is
conformant provided two implementations agree on the default below until
the item is revised. Items 1–5 are asserted non-semantic.

1. **Binary encoding.** Default: none; §16 text is the only interchange
   form.
2. **Gate-name registry.** Default: the names of §4.4 only; an unknown
   gate name is a load error.
3. **Further device models.** Default: the four of §7.3; an `effect` on
   any other device is `FAULT_EFFECT_UNMODELLED`.
4. **Mechanisation of §14.** Default: none; the reference interpreter is
   the oracle.
5. **Memory layout of the opaque C objects.** Default: opaque; only the
   listed functions touch them.
6. **Measurement-based cleanup** (§6.4): the normative fixup, its
   controlled variant and its certificate kind. Default: unitary cleanup
   (`reverse`); `capabilities.mbu` is `false`.
7. **Expansion library** (§11): the gate-level expansion below row
   level. Default: the library `ref` published by `bennett-isa-spec`
   (ripple-carry adders and comparators, linear `inrange` scans,
   one-hot address dispatch, clean scratch released in reverse order);
   a schedule that names no `expansion` binds `ref` at the ISA version
   in the object's header, with the full-library envelope hash published
   for that version in §11.1. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-11.1-P3, ISA-11.1-P4]
   For a 0.9 object this is exactly
   `{"name":"ref","version":"0.9","hash":"5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197"}`,
   equally for omission and an explicit binding. [deps: ISA-11.1-P3, ISA-11.1-P4, ISA-11.1]
   §11.1 fixes its schema, ordinals, scratch identities, empty removable
   sets and deterministic decomposition algorithms. [deps: ISA-11.1]
   The bound catalog is published as `spec/ref-0.9.json`. The worked
   `ctl` action fixture (zsw.4) is in
   `conformance/fixtures/controlled-action-09/`: `controlled.bisa`,
   `target.json`, `leaves.json`, `schedule.json`, `frame.json` and
   `certificate.json`. It contains one exact controlled-X leaf, starts
   at time 0 and ends at time 1, with two live fast-class interface wires
   and no workspace. Its README records derivation and the pinned 0.8
   compatibility check separately from the 0.9 bindings.
8. **Enclosure procedure for transcendental entries** (§12.1). Default:
   Taylor series of sin and cos to the first term whose Lagrange
   remainder bound is below 2^−(`precision_bits` + 2), argument reduced
   modulo 2π with an outward-rounded enclosure of π.
9. **Default `epsilon_share`** (§8.1, §11) when a schedule assigns none.
   Default: the exact rational `bindings.epsilon / N`, where N is the
   number of gate operations in the bound-library expansion of one
   invocation of `bindings.region`, including every nested invocation,
   that have no exact native match as defined below. [deps: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3, ISA-11-P2]
   For this count only, an exact native match is a `gates` entry whose
   opcode, classes, control range and parameters cover the operation
   and whose `epsilon` expression is independent of `eps` and evaluates
   to zero; compute this test before the requested-accuracy filter,
   without selecting an achieved-error entry using the default share. [deps: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3]
   N counts the gate operations before target scheme expansion and
   includes operations with explicit shares as well as omitted shares;
   the root accuracy is `bindings.epsilon`, not a nested region's
   separately requested accuracy. [deps: ISA-11-P2, ISA-11-P3, ISA-8.2, ISA-8.3]
   Gates with an exact native match take a default share of zero; if
   N=0 all default shares are zero and no division is performed. [deps: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3]
   The resolved share is represented exactly as a reduced pair (p,q)
   of nonnegative numerator and positive denominator, with zero
   represented by (0,1); it is passed as that rational value to every
   §8.1 comparison, expression and accuracy bucket, without rounding
   to a finite decimal. [deps: ISA-8.1]
   This is a resolved value, not an inserted `epsilon_share` field;
   existing decimal schedule fields and the hashed schedule bytes are
   unchanged by default resolution. [deps: ISA-11-P3, ISA-9.2]
   Explicit shares still supply `eps` by their exact value and the
   §8.1 requested-versus-achieved filter and achieved-error charge
   still apply to every resolved share. [deps: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3]
   *(non-normative witness)* At `bindings.epsilon: "0.1"`, three gates
   lacking exact native matches give N=3 and default (1,30), even if
   one gate has an explicit share; a fourth, exact-native gate takes
   zero and is excluded from N. [deps: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3, ISA-11-P2]
   With no such inexact gates N=0. [deps: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3, ISA-11-P2]
   Omitting expansion on a 0.9 object selects the full-library hash in

                return err("§12.3 K13", "standalone claim requires invocation");
            }
        }
        // §12.1–12.2: domain/name bindings, executable subjects and acyclic
        // indices need the enclosing function/claim list, beyond the grammar.
        match c.get("kind")?.string()? {
            "exhaustive" => {
                domain(c.get("domain")?, f)?;
                if c.get("expected")? == &Json::String("oracle".into()) {
                    subject(c.get("subject")?)?;
                }
            }
            "sampled" => {
                domain(c.get("domain")?, f)?;
                if !c
                    .get("sampler")?
                    .get("seed")?
                    .array()?
                    .iter()
                    .any(|w| w.string().is_ok_and(|s| s.bytes().any(|b| b != b'0')))
                {
                    return err("§12.1", "all-zero sampler seed");
                }
            }
            "structural" => {
                for claim in c.get("subclaims")?.array()? {
                    index(claim, position)?;
                }
            }
            "numerical" | "channel" => {
                subject(c.get("subject")?)?;
                if c.get("precision_bits")?.uint()? == 0 {
                    return err("§12.1", "precision must be positive");
                }
            }
            _ => return err("§12.1", "unknown certificate claim kind"),
        }
        Ok(())
    }

#[cfg(test)]
mod r20_tests {
    use super::*;
    #[test]
    fn r20_region_domain_extremes() {
        let values = [0u64, 1, (1 << 63) - 1, 1 << 63, u64::MAX - 1, u64::MAX];
        let mut cases = 0;
        for n in values {
            for path in ["A", "W", "index", "lo", "hi"] {
                let (a, w, index, lo, hi) = match path {
                    "A" => (n, 1, 0, 0, 1),
                    "W" => (0, n, 0, 0, 1),
                    "index" => (1, 1, n, 0, 1),
                    "lo" => (0, 1, 0, n, n),
                    _ => (0, 1, 0, 0, n),
                };
                let mut f = Function::from_signature("o", &Json::parse(r#"{"abi":"0.8","args":[],"fault":4,"in":[],"kind":"oracle","mem":[],"tgt":8,"tier":"q"}"#).unwrap()).unwrap();
                f.params[3].push(crate::Param {
                    name: "r".into(),
                    ty: Type::Region(a, w),
                    role: Some("in".into()),
                });
                let meta = |n: u64| {
                    if n <= 9_007_199_254_740_991 {
                        n.to_string()
                    } else {
                        format!("\"{n}\"")
                    }
                };
                let d = Json::parse(&format!(r#"{{"bundles":[],"borrowed":[],"regions":[{{"name":"r","cells":[{{"index":{},"lo":{},"hi":{}}}]}}]}}"#,meta(index),meta(lo),meta(hi))).unwrap();
                let admit = match path {
                    "A" => true,
                    "W" => n > 0,
                    "index" => n < 2,
                    _ => n < 2,
                };
                let result = std::panic::catch_unwind(|| domain(&d, &f)).expect("no domain panic");
                if admit {
                    result.unwrap();
                } else {
                    let e = result.unwrap_err().to_string();
                    assert!(
                        e.contains('§') && !e.contains("host limit"),
                        "{path}={n}: {e}"
                    );
                }
                cases += 1;
            }
        }
        assert_eq!(cases, 30);

from math import gcd
import hashlib
import re

def scalar(n):
    return (F(n), F(0), F(0), F(0))

ZERO, ONE = scalar(0), scalar(1)
Z = (F(0), F(1), F(0), F(0))

def add(a, b):
    return tuple(x + y for x, y in zip(a, b))

def neg(a):
    return tuple(-x for x in a)

def mul(a, b):
    out = [F(0)] * 7
    for i in range(4):
        for j in range(4):
            out[i+j] += a[i] * b[j]
    for k in range(6, 3, -1):
        out[k-4] -= out[k]
    return tuple(out[:4])

def conj(a):
    out = [a[0], F(0), F(0), F(0)]
    for k in range(1, 4):
        out[4-k] -= a[k]
    return tuple(out)

I = mul(Z, Z)
TINV = conj(Z)
HFACTOR = (F(0), F(1, 2), F(0), F(-1, 2))
assert mul(HFACTOR, HFACTOR) == scalar(F(1, 2))
assert mul(Z, TINV) == ONE
assert mul(I, I) == neg(ONE)

def compact(state):
    return {i: a for i, a in state.items() if a != ZERO}

def accumulate(state, i, a):
    state[i] = add(state.get(i, ZERO), a)

def enabled(i, controls):
    return all((i >> c) & 1 for c in controls)

def hadamard(state, wire, controls=()):
    out = {}
    mask = 1 << wire
    for i, a in state.items():
        if not enabled(i, controls):
            accumulate(out, i, a)
            continue
        a = mul(a, HFACTOR)
        low = i & ~mask
        accumulate(out, low, a)
        accumulate(out, low | mask, neg(a) if i & mask else a)
    return compact(out)

def flip(state, target, controls=()):
    out = {}
    for i, a in state.items():
        j = i ^ (1 << target) if enabled(i, controls) else i
        accumulate(out, j, a)
    return compact(out)

def phase(state, target, factor, controls=()):
    return compact({i: mul(a, factor) if (i >> target) & 1
                    and enabled(i, controls) else a
                    for i, a in state.items()})

# Bit positions: a=0, b=1, g=2, y=3, outer q=4.
C = [('h', 2), ('t', 2), ('cx', (0, 2)), ('tdg', 2),
     ('cx', (1, 2)), ('t', 2), ('cx', (0, 2)),
     ('tdg', 2), ('h', 2), ('sdg', 2)]
adj = {'h': 'h', 'cx': 'cx', 't': 'tdg', 'tdg': 't',
       's': 'sdg', 'sdg': 's'}
CDAG = [(adj[op], args) for op, args in reversed(C)]

def sequence(state, gates, controls=()):
    for op, args in gates:
        if op == 'h':
            state = hadamard(state, args, controls)
        elif op == 'cx':
            state = flip(state, args[1], (args[0],) + controls)
        else:
            factor = {'t': Z, 'tdg': TINV, 's': I, 'sdg': neg(I)}[op]
            state = phase(state, args, factor, controls)
    return state

for a in range(2):
    for b in range(2):
        i = a | (b << 1)
        assert sequence({i: ONE}, C) == {i | ((a*b) << 2): ONE}
        dirty_phase = I if (a, b) == (0, 1) else neg(I)
        assert sequence({i | 4: ONE}, C) == {
            i | ((1 ^ (a*b)) << 2): dirty_phase}

def expected(state, controlled, closed):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    state = flip(state, 3, (0, 1) + outer)
    return hadamard(state, 0, outer) if closed else state

def branches(state, controlled=False, closed=False, correction='correct'):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    state = sequence(state, C, outer)
    state = flip(state, 3, (2,) + outer)
    if closed:
        state = hadamard(state, 0, outer)
    state = hadamard(state, 2)  # Unconditional X-basis measurement.
    result = []
    for m in range(2):
        branch = {i: a for i, a in state.items() if ((i >> 2) & 1) == m}
        if m:
            branch = flip(branch, 2)  # Reset measured private wire.
            transport = closed and correction != 'stale'
            if transport:
                branch = hadamard(branch, 0, outer)
            if correction != 'none':
                controls = (0, 1) + (() if correction == 'uncontrolled' else outer)
                branch = {i: neg(a) if enabled(i, controls) else a
                          for i, a in branch.items()}
            if transport:
                branch = hadamard(branch, 0, outer)
        result.append(compact(branch))
    return result

branch_columns = 0
for controlled in (False, True):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    for i in range(32):
        if i & 4:
            continue
        start = {i: ONE}
        unitary = sequence(start, C, outer)
        unitary = flip(unitary, 3, (2,) + outer)
        unitary = sequence(unitary, CDAG, outer)
        assert unitary == expected(start, controlled, False)
        for closed in (False, True):
            want = {j: mul(a, HFACTOR)
                    for j, a in expected(start, controlled, closed).items()}
            for branch in branches(start, controlled, closed):
                assert branch == compact(want)
                branch_columns += 1
assert branch_columns == 128

def density(states):
    out = {}
    for state in states:
        for i, a in state.items():
            for j, b in state.items():
                accumulate(out, (i, j), mul(a, conj(b)))
    return compact(out)

superposition = {0: ONE}
for wire in (0, 1, 4):
    superposition = hadamard(superposition, wire)
for controlled, closed, correction in [
        (False, False, 'none'), (True, False, 'uncontrolled'),
        (False, True, 'stale')]:
    actual = density(branches(superposition, controlled, closed, correction))
    ideal = density([expected(superposition, controlled, closed)])
    assert actual != ideal, correction

# Imported model, unit conversion, sequence/parallel accounting.
def model(d):
    return 3*2*d, 2*d*d, F(1, 10) * F(1, 100)**((d+1)//2)
assert model(5) == (30, 50, F(1, 10**7))
assert model(3) == (18, 18, F(1, 100000))
assert F(30, 10**9) == F(3, 10**8)
assert 3*30*50 == 4500
assert (3*30, 50) == (90, 50)  # Sequential time/peak.
assert (30, 3*50) == (30, 150)  # Parallel time/peak.

# Width dispatch, persistent versus reversed load state.
assert (1 ^ 0, 5 ^ 3) == (1, 6)
def later_run(reverse):
    memory, bump = 7, 17  # A stored 7 and allocated at 16.
    if reverse:
        memory, bump = 0, 16
    return memory, bump
assert later_run(False) == (7, 17)
assert later_run(True) == (0, 16)

# Fixed-image coverage; hashing raw bytes differs from hashing hex text.
of every borrowed bit; no conversion to class any is permitted.

**Angles and quantum witnesses (K20).** An `angle` actual is a bound angle
formal or a finite binary64 value. Reusing §12.3's bit-pattern encoding:
`f64` is `0x` followed by exactly 16 lowercase hex digits of its IEEE 754
binary64 bit pattern, most significant nibble first (so −0 and NaN payloads
are not lost). Angles additionally reject NaN and infinities (§3.1).
Only `rx`, `ry`, `rz` have `params`, an array containing exactly one angle;
other §4.4 gates have no angle parameters. No recipe arithmetic on angles
is permitted. `inverse` negates each rotation angle by toggling bit 63,
including signed zero, and exchanges s/sdg and t/tdg as §5.3 requires.

For h, phase gates s/sdg/t/tdg/cz, and rotations, correctness is equality
of the §14.2 unitary (using §4.4 matrices), column by column on the full
computational basis of all recipe interface wires, with scratch initially
clean and finally clean. Arbitrary targets, controls and borrowed inputs
are included. This full-basis unitary is the witness extending T3's
basis argument; non-permutation leaves MUST NOT be certified by a basis
permutation simulator. Exact algebraic amplitude checks apply to Clifford+T
leaves. For arbitrary binary64 angles a numerical checker must state its
round-trip criterion and error bounds; numerical agreement is evidence,
not a proof of ε=0 for arbitrary transcendental matrix entries.

# Bead requests for the orchestrator

The sol-checker runner permits writes only in checker/** and docs/checker.md.
No sibling bead database was changed. File these in bennett-isa-spec with
labels defect, isa, open; these are contract questions, not assumptions
used to accept a certificate.

1. **§12.1 cannot construct a native approximation's realised operator from §8.3 metadata.**
   Determining evidence: a `gates` entry supplies opcode, classes, controls,
   epsilon and costs, but no actual matrix/channel, while §12.1 requires
   building the realised U/channel independently. Distinct native channels
   with the same advertised epsilon/cost are indistinguishable to a checker.
   Proposed exact addition after §8.3 native gate schema: "A native entry
   with nonzero epsilon MUST bind a versioned realisation model containing
   its matrix or Kraus operators as exact expressions or certified interval
   enclosures, with parameter and control conventions fixed. The model's
   canonical bytes are included in target_hash. Numerical and channel
   checkers MUST construct the realised operation from this model; epsilon
   alone is not an operator description. An absent model leaves those
   obligations unchecked and never licenses reference self-comparison."
   Checker currently handles exact native §4.4 gates; nonzero/expressed
   native epsilon is explicitly unchecked.

2. **§12 publishes no certificate-checker rejection-code registry.**
   Determining evidence: §12 specifies rejection obligations but not a
   stable error-code map; §11 separately names E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE.
   Proposed exact addition after §12.3 schema: "A checker MUST provide a
   deterministic report with the failed obligation's section, certificate
   path and a documented stable diagnostic code. Checker-defined E_CERT_*
   codes are permitted; mandated executor codes such as
   E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE retain their specified meaning."
   Tests pin this checker's documented E_CERT_* codes, not an invented
   normative registry.

3. **§12.3 fault_rate has no explicit invocation binding.**
   Determining evidence: claims bind arguments/budgets individually; a
   standalone region may carry several claims with different invocation
   values, yet verification.fault_rate has only domain and counts.
   Proposed exact addition to fault_rate schema: "invocation: {args,
   budget}"; add: "Every fault_rate recount binds its own invocation using
   the encodings and validation of K13. Counts are recomputed on that
   invocation and the declared domain; no claim's binding is inherited."
   Scheduled certificates could instead specify a per-context count list;
   that alternative requires an explicit spec decision. Current oracle
   fault counts remain unchecked rather than guessing one claim's budget.

4. **§12.3 does not define resource-report aggregation for repeated region contexts.**
   Determining witness: root `a(t,c)` runs `action_call @flip (t) () () ()`
   then `ctl c action_call @flip (t) () () ()`; flip is `x t`.
   An exact native X entry matching total controls 0..1 and duration 1
   gives the same flip closure two contexts: (forward,0) has one live wire,
   time 1, qubit_second 1, peak_qubits 1; (forward,1) has two live wires,
   time 1, qubit_second 2, peak_qubits 2. The certificate's region.resources
   and region.gates_used are flat maps, unlike error/cleanup, and §12 does
   not say which context they describe, whether they are bounds, or whether
   they aggregate calls. Guessing max/sum/one arbitrary context would give
   incompatible answers. Classical arguments can make this vary even at
   the same variant/control key.
   Proposed exact addition to the region schema:
   `resource_contexts?: [ { context_key, resources, gates_used, estimate? } ]`.
   Add after the §12.3 schema: "When a region has exactly one invocation
   context, its resources/gates_used maps report that invocation. When it
   has more than one invocation context, resource_contexts MUST contain
   exactly one record for each §12.1 context, in the same enumeration order,
   with exact §8.2 accounting and §10.1 encodings where estimate is present.
   The unkeyed resources/gates_used maps remain required for compatibility
   but discharge no per-context resource obligation in that case. Checkers
   MUST reject missing, duplicate or mismatched context records, rather than
   interpreting unkeyed maps as sums or maxima."
   Decision needed from the spec owner: adopt this per-context encoding or
   define a different explicit aggregation. Current checker verifies the
   published single-context reports completely, but repeated-context flat
   reports remain unchecked. Numerical checks still enumerate every context.

5. **§12.1 adjoint_table lacks a binding to the forward proof/body.**
   Determining witness: §16.3 example 3 explicitly says the reverse borrow
   sequence `cx b t; cx x b; cx b t; cx x b` is not a translation-lemma
   frame and is covered by adjoint_table. The structural payload fixes its
   witness to `{}` and supplies only integer subclaim indices. §12.3 stores
   claims per region; variants are opaque producer strings, compared bytewise,

     1	# Checker question 1 — realised native operators
     2	
     3	Proposal for council review; source request 1 in
     4	`../bennett-conformance/checker/.tmp/CROSS_REPO_BEADS.md`.
     5	
     6	## Defect and North Star scope
     7	
     8	ISA §8.3's native `gates` entries contain opcode, classes, controls,
     9	parameters, cost and epsilon; §8.1 supplies arithmetic expressions, not
    10	an operator description. §12.1 requires the checker to build realised U
    11	or a channel independently for numerical/channel claims, and §4.4
    12	defines only the ideal opcode plus an error obligation. Cost and an
    13	advertised distance bound do not identify the realised operation.
    14	This is a genuine S0 contract gap for independent native approximation
    15	verification (rubric C3/C5), not grounds to accept reference self-comparison.
    16	
    17	North Star §§4.4/5.1 part 2 promises independent phase-sensitive and
    18	channel verification. Returning unchecked for an unsupported model is
    19	honest checker evidence handling, but is not the missing model mechanism.
    20	Exact logical native gates retain their declared §4.4 matrices; algorithms
    21	with nonzero approximation remain in scope.
    22	
    23	## Alternatives and worked witness
    24	
    25	Take one native X entry with fixed advertised epsilon=2 and time=1.
    26	Realisation A is X and realisation B is −X. Both meet that advertised
    27	operator-norm bound, cost, opcode and class metadata; they produce the
    28	same uncontrolled channel. Their controlled extensions differ: on
    29	`|+>_q|0>_t` they produce `(00+11)/sqrt(2)` and `(00−11)/sqrt(2)`,
    30	orthogonal states. An entry with no realisation bytes cannot tell the
    31	checker which process to compare, even though both targets have the same
    32	current JSON/hash. Certifying actual bound 0 under coherent control is
    33	valid only for A. A conservative declared bound 2 can be composed as an
    34	assumption, but that is not independent reconstruction of a tighter claim.
    35	
    36	| Alternative | Assessment |
    37	|---|---|
    38	| Treat every native entry as its ideal opcode | Circular self-comparison for approximate entries. |
    39	| Trust advertised epsilon | Explicit target assumption only; cannot discharge §12.1 independent reconstruction. |
    40	| Hash-bound matrix/channel model (recommended) | Supplies the actual operation, controls and conventions needed for checking. |
    41	| Model approximations as explicit existing schemes | Already works when every leaf is exact and the scheme lists the actual circuit; cannot describe an opaque approximate native leaf alone. |
    42	
    43	## Recommended exact spec diff
    44	
    45	Add to §8.3 gate entries `realisation_model?: {version,kind,arity,basis,
    46	parameter_bindings,control_models,data}` and append:
    47	
    48	```text
    49	A native entry with nonzero or expression-valued epsilon MUST supply a
    50	versioned realisation_model. Its canonical bytes are part of target_hash.
    51	kind is unitary or channel. basis lists ordered operand bits; matrices use
    52	the §12.1 computational basis with inherited controls first. Parameters
    53	bind by their exact §4.4 angle values and declared operand order. data
    54	contains the actual complex matrix, or an ordered Kraus list, as exact
    55	expressions over rationals, i, pi, sqrt, exp, sin and cos, or certified
    56	interval enclosures of an identified exact model. Real/imaginary rational
    57	constants are numerator/positive-denominator decimal-string pairs.
    58	Intervals are closed rational endpoints; enclosures need an independently
    59	checkable certificate for the enclosed exact operation and its unitary
    60	or completely-positive trace-preserving property. An unverified box of
    61	numbers alone is not an operation model.
    62	
    63	For each admitted total control count, control_models binds the actual
    64	controlled implementation, including environment/measurement conventions.
    65	A unitary model may use the exact controlled-U construction including its
    66	global phase. A channel is not given a coherent controlled extension by
    67	controlling arbitrary Kraus operators; it requires an explicit instrument
    68	or dilation/control model. The checker resolves every parameter and
    69	control context and constructs the realised operation from that model,
    70	checks dimensions and normalization, and verifies the claimed distance
    71	with §12.1's interval algorithm. The epsilon field remains a separate
    72	accuracy/matching obligation and is not the operator definition.
    73	Exact entries may omit the model and then denote §4.4's exact opcode,
    74	including its exact controlled form. A supplied model must agree with
    75	that contract. No absent model licenses ideal-vs-ideal self-comparison.
    76	```
    77	
    78	For legacy targets lacking a model, preserve parse/execution compatibility
    79	but record affected numerical/channel obligations as unchecked (missing
    80	realisation model). Do not issue a checked executable certificate from
    81	those missing obligations. The new model version is an explicit target
    82	format extension; unknown model versions receive unsupported-model
    83	reporting, never a guessed matrix.
    84	
    85	## Hashes, catalog and consumers
    86	
    87	New native-model bytes change target_hash and every target-bound schedule
    88	and certificate. No canonical source or reference catalog changes. Target
    89	authors, scheduler and checker must resolve the same model and controlled
    90	variant; a simulator using ideal opcodes alone is not a physical native
    91	model checker. Physical stochastic failure probabilities remain separate
    92	from logical approximation epsilon (see fd0).
    93	
    94	## Conformance vectors and verification
    95	
    96	Use X/−X with an outer control and compare matrix units/Choi operators;
    97	test uncontrolled equal channels but unequal controlled processes. Exact
    98	X without a model remains valid. Missing approximate model, wrong arity,
    99	non-normalized Kraus list, parameter/control mismatch, a model-hash change
   100	and a rigorously inconclusive interval claim must not pass the obligation.
   101	The Bell-state sign witness is checked in
   102	[contract-verification](2026-11-02-contract-verification.md). No native
   103	target checker was executed for this note.
   104	
   105	## Release and after acceptance
   106	
   107	Recommend a **0.9 K contract repair with a versioned model schema**, or a
   108	0.10 target extension with this current gap explicitly blocking independent
   109	approximation claims. The checker question is already the cross-repo bead
   110	filing request; the orchestrator should file/retain it in this repo with
   111	this exact missing mechanism. Council must accept the model and migration
   112	rules before a consumer advertises checked approximate native support.

from fractions import Fraction as F

def mul(a,b):
    return [[sum(x*y for x,y in zip(row,col)) for col in zip(*b)] for row in a]
def trans(a):
    return [list(row) for row in zip(*a)]
def neg(a):
    return [[-x for x in row] for row in a]
def chan(u,a):
    return mul(mul(u,a),trans(u))
def controlled(u):
    return [[(int(i==j) if i<2 and j<2 else u[i-2][j-2] if i>=2 and j>=2 else 0) for j in range(4)] for i in range(4)]

for i in range(2):
    for j in range(2):
        unit=[[int((r,c)==(i,j)) for c in range(2)] for r in range(2)]
        assert chan(x,unit)==chan(neg(x),unit)
rho=[[F(int(i in [0,2] and j in [0,2]),2) for j in range(4)] for i in range(4)]

assert plus[0][3]==F(1,2) and minus[0][3]==-F(1,2)
assert sum(mul(plus,minus)[i][i] for i in range(4))==0
print('PASS exact matrix units: Ch(X)=Ch(-X); controlled Bell coherences = +1/2 and -1/2; overlap = 0')

{"capabilities":{"classical_step_time":1,"max_parallel":"unbounded","max_predicate_depth":"unbounded","mbu":false,"measurement":{"feedforward_latency":0,"mid_circuit":false}},"classes":{"fast":{"capacity":2,"dirty_rate":1,"qubit_second":1}},"gates":[{"classes":["fast"],"controls":{"max":1,"min":1},"cost":{"time":1},"epsilon":0,"op":"x"}],"imports":{},"objective":{"weighted":{"time":1}},"ptr_widths_supported":[32],"qram":null,"resources":{"peak_qubits":{"kind":"peak","unit":"qubit"},"qubit_second":{"kind":"occupancy","unit":"qubit_second"},"time":{"kind":"time","unit":"second"}},"schemes":[],"tables":{}}# v2v — frame descriptors for every realised invocation context

Council proposal for `b-isa-spec-v2v` (P1).

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §12.1 “Invocation contexts” includes every reached invocation,
including defaults, with resolved arguments, budget and choices. §12.3
stores one region record per closure hash and requires one B5 frame.
§9.4 requires each slot's concrete target class and substitutes parameters
and budget into width constants before forming c-only expressions.
The same callee can receive A in one call and B in another; no single
descriptor can express both. A `(variant,controls)` key alone also fails
when those values agree. This is a C4/C5 contract defect.

North Star §§4.2/4.4/5.1 requires freely nested regions and independently
checkable schedules/certificates. Refusing repeated mixed-class calls
would narrow this machine. [4ta](b-isa-spec-4ta.md) describes a different
case: a single slot fragmented within one context. Both repairs compose.

## Alternatives and worked witness

Use the source from `../bennett-sched/tests/pipeline.rs`, test
`context_varying_frame_classes_are_explicitly_deferred_instead_of_misreported`:
root `pair(t,u)` calls the same one-bit `flip` on t and then u; flip is X.
Place t in A and u in B, capacity 1 each. Each call is forward with zero
inherited controls and the same closure hash. Callee frame one has
`mut:0,class:A,width:1`; callee frame two has B instead.

For all four root basis inputs, the result is `(t XOR 1,u XOR 1)`.
At unit duration/rate the root time is 2 and each root interface bit is
live for both slots: occupancy[A]=occupancy[B]=2, peaks 1 each.
The per-invocation callee views have duration 1 and just their respective
class. Neither copying A into both records nor taking a class wildcard
describes the realised placement. This witness needs no heterogeneous
slot and no nonzero epsilon.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Duplicate region records or rename the callee | Breaks closure-keyed identity or unnecessarily changes the source. |
| Use one wildcard/maximal frame | Cannot verify concrete classes and actual widths. |
| Exact context-indexed frames, uniform shorthand retained | Describes both calls and keeps old uniform certificates. Recommended. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Replace the §12.3 region `frame (required)` entry by:

```text
frame?: <§9.4 descriptor>,
frame_contexts?: [{context_key:<§12.1 key>, frame:<§9.4 descriptor>}]
```

Append:

```text
Each scheduled region record contains exactly one of frame and
frame_contexts. frame is a descriptor valid for every reached invocation
of that region in the bound root realisation, after instantiating c for
each invocation. It may be used only if all its concrete classes and
resolved non-c-dependent widths describe every invocation truthfully.
frame_contexts contains exactly one record for each reached invocation,
in increasing bytewise order of the JCS encoding of context_key. The
key is exactly §12.1's fully resolved invocation chain; no wildcard,
variant/control-only key, omitted default-covered call or repeated key
is admitted. The root invocation has key []. Resolve its variant,
controls, arguments, budget, placements and expansion choices from the
bound invocation tree, never from record order or a guessed root budget.

Select the context's descriptor before substituting c and check its
slots, classes, widths, allocation identities and lifetimes against
that invocation. Inherited controls remain caller-owned and separate
from callee slots. Distinct contexts have distinct dynamic frame
identities even when their static descriptor id strings agree. Identical
descriptor bytes do not authorize shared physical storage; §9.4's
live-range and disjointness rules remain required. A standalone reference
record uses frame for its explicitly bound invocation or a descriptor
proved uniform over its declared invocation scope.
```

Here `context_key` uses the same spelling as the first-pass resource and
fault proposals. Their arrays and `frame_contexts` must cover the same
reached context set when applicable, rather than enumerate independently.
The originating bead's field spelling `context` is superseded by this
consistent proposed spelling; neither is published yet.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

No callee clone, source/interface/closure hash or catalog change is
required. Certificate bytes change. Existing schedules already distinguish
the two source call points; no new call edge is invented. Schedulers,
specialisers and checkers must retain full context keys across linked and
default-covered calls. Estimator frames and resource reports must select
the same context. A uniformly valid c-expression stays usable as `frame`;
the repair must not force needless duplication of uniform certificates.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Use the pair/flip case; check both class records and all four outputs,
then inverse restoration. Reject two A records, a missing default call,
duplicate/foreign key, both `frame` and `frame_contexts`, neither field,
# 95w — native models: incremental binding requirements

Second-pass proposal for `b-isa-spec-95w`. The defect, X/−X witness,
alternatives and model proposal are already derived in
[the first-pass native-model note](../2026-11-02-contract-checker-native-model.md).
That proposal is incorporated by reference; this note adds only the
context, exact-entry and publication boundaries it leaves to settle.

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §8.3 supplies native opcode/cost/epsilon metadata but no realised
operator. §12.1 requires independent construction of U/channel at every
resolved context, with controls first in its basis. An advertised bound
does not determine U. This is the same C3/C5 gap, not a second issue.
North Star §5.1 part 2's independent process comparison remains required
for approximate native gates, including coherent control; an unchecked
legacy obligation does not deliver that capability.

## Alternatives and worked witness

Use the first pass's alternatives and concrete X/−X controlled-interference
witness without another model-schema derivation here.

Additional exact-entry witness: the §8.1 expression `{"op":"-",
"args":[1,1]}` is identically zero. Its syntactic expression form does
not make an otherwise exact X entry approximate. Conversely an expression
that evaluates to zero for one parameter/control context and nonzero for
another needs the model in the latter context. Do not turn “expression-
valued epsilon” into a new, broader exact-entry rejection rule.

## Recommended exact spec diff

Apply the first-pass schema/model text, replacing its opening requirement
and adding these precise qualifications in §8.3:

```text
A native entry that can evaluate to nonzero epsilon in an admitted
resolved context must bind the versioned realisation_model described
here. For a context whose evaluated epsilon is zero, the operation is
exactly §4.4's opcode, including global phase and controlled form;
an omitted model denotes that operation. Expression syntax alone does
not remove this exact-entry convention. Any supplied model must agree
with every zero-epsilon context and satisfy the advertised bound in
every other context in which it is used.

Resolve model parameters, operand bit order and total effective control
context from the same matched target entry and invocation used for
cost/error accounting. The model's operand-to-wire map must explicitly
relate its declared basis to §12.1's controls-first allocation basis;
permutation of matrix indices is checked, never inferred from a vendor
name. A controlled model includes phase relative to the disabled branch.
For a channel use the specified controlled instrument/dilation, not
an arbitrary coherently controlled Kraus representation.

An externally stored model binds canonical bytes by digest and a
published model-format version; those bindings enter the target's JCS
bytes. A location/URL alone is not model identity. A model change thus
invalidates the target-bound schedule and certificate. A legacy target
without the required model remains executable under its declared target
assumptions, but the affected independent approximation obligation is
unchecked and cannot be relabelled checked by reference self-comparison.
```

The first-pass model grammar, exact/interval certificate requirements and
dimension/normalisation checks still apply. Unsupported model mechanics
must name the missing format, not remove approximate algorithms from
scope. Do not publish this prose without a versioned model schema and
positive/negative bytes accepted by independent consumers.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

The first pass's hash analysis stands: new model bindings change
`target_hash` and dependent schedules/certificates, not source closures
or the ref catalog. Add cache tests for changed **model digest** at equal
opcode, epsilon and cost. Producers, target authors and checkers must
agree on controlled and parameter-dependent models, including models
reached through target schemes and default-only calls.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Reuse the first-pass X/−X vector. Add syntactic-zero epsilon, an expression
with zero and positive contexts, matrix basis reordering, a same-location
changed digest, disabled-control phase, and a non-trace-preserving Kraus
list. The exact Bell-state overlap and rational expression are checked
in [INDEX](INDEX.md). No new native-model consumer is run or claimed.
Origin read: `../bennett-conformance/checker/.tmp/CROSS_REPO_BEADS.md`, item 1.

## Release and route

Recommend the **same 0.9 K plus versioned target-model repair** as the
first pass. If schema publication waits for 0.10, retain 95w as the
explicit current blocker for independent approximate-native checking.
Do not create a duplicate bead or re-open exact §4.4 gate semantics.
